- [ ] **Task 19: cross-host and sync-folder validation** (spec §3.13). Must measure or confirm:
  - [x] macOS suites re-run on 3e284dc: 569 Rust, 85 JS, gates, e2e, clippy clean
  - [x] iCloud sync rows (one device): temp-then-rename safe, hydration matches the code, `sync:icloud`; conflict copies need a second device

  - NAS matrix per host pair: lease races (0 double holders, 0 early takeovers, 0 applied stale writes, 0 id reuse), network loss with writes in flight, real or emulated skew, follow latency ≤ 3 s + cache window
    - [x] Windows↔Linux: all rows 0 critical on the final code (spec §3.13 item 2)
    - [x] Mac↔Linux before the fix: found the macOS smbfs 30–60 s directory cache (unsafe 10 s ordering-rule wait); fixed (65 s wait + listing refresh, 3e284dc); network poll 1 s, churn export delay 5 s
    - [x] Mac↔Linux and Mac↔Windows on the final code: all rows 0 critical (spec §3.13 item 2)
  - [x] items 4, 5, 7, 8, 13, 14, 15 and most of 10 on Linux↔Windows SMB (spec §3.13); fixed the cifs cached-mtime probe read and verify flagging locked segments as corrupt
  - mtime source on SMB (server- vs client-set; Samba 2 s write-time delay; cifs deferred close); claims judged by client-set mtime under skew > 45 s
  - clock skew; suspend/resume of a holder (wakes fenced); Windows claim invisibility race (up to 9.9 s)
  - `File::try_lock`/`lock_shared` behaviour on Linux, Windows, macOS (`lease.lock`, `db.lock`, `server-id.lock`, restart shortcut)

  - mount-root rules (macOS `/Volumes`, Windows mapped drives)
  - storage-kind detection on real iCloud (incl. Desktop & Documents), OneDrive (KFM, SharePoint), Google Drive (`G:`), Dropbox (File Provider), rclone/FUSE, WSL2
  - Dropbox conflict names (`conflicted copy`, `Case Conflict`, `Selective Sync Conflict`), Google Drive patterns
  - transient error lists (`fsio::is_transient`); SQLite `immutable=1` URI forms and backup fsync on Windows
  - compaction deletes on SMB (open handles), sync folders delivering deletions before packs
  - sync rows: temp-then-rename upload, placeholder hydration, sync gate after a confirmed takeover
  - [x] daily backups: macOS local date right in five zones; Windows `FileTimeToLocalFileTime` used today's bias for any date (2 of 6 DST-day instants a day off) → `SystemTimeToTzSpecificLocalTimeEx`, tested with injected zones (bdc94e5, spec §3.13 item 17)
  - [x] status after `lease.json` is deleted under a holder: the holder now reports `LEASE_LOST`, others `LEASE_HELD` (947aad5; also after a restart with the server-data lock free, takeover wording, sync `--confirm`: 34bd3c7; spec §3.13 item 18)
- **5E (from 7A Task 11):** register `review.verdict` at version 1 with `source: agent` among its sources and the fields 7 asks for (sp7 §1.7 item 5): `verdict` (`accepted|rejected|merged|dismissed|changesRequested|undone|reverted|abandoned`), `txIds`, `by` (`user|agent|critic|policy`), `via` (`undo|revert|archive|merge|critic|autoMerge`), `itemKind` (`none` for the tracker's), `sessionId?`, `openS`, training-only `reason`. Nothing else is needed: the outcome tracker (`apps/finisherd/src/agent/outcomes.ts`) looks the kind up with fin-node's `kindVersion("review.verdict", "agent")` before every outcome and emits `OutcomeVerdict` (`{verdict, txIds: [txId], by, via, itemKind: "none", sessionId, openS}`, ctx `{project, txId, actor: {kind: agent, session}}`) once it answers a version; until then outcomes are only in `session_txs.outcome` and `session_tx_outcomes` (`emitted: 0`; no backfill of earlier ones). 5E's own `review.merge` is not a history RPC, so the tracker never double-counts it; if 5E merges through `history.merge` RPCs instead, it must tell the tracker (the after hook sees the tag only). `by: policy`/`via: autoMerge` wait for sub-project 10

### Follow-ups from 4A
- [ ] fin-store holds the store mutex across folder I/O in `tick` (import reads, export writes): take it only around DB transactions (read/verify segments outside, apply under; write packs outside). A big follower import delays short calls today. (`VACUUM INTO` on a second connection: done for the daily backup, `fin-node::auto`; an automatic compaction step still holds the mutex)
- [ ] `ProjectStatus` (and the verify, rebuild and compact reports) still `Schema.Unknown` in contracts: generate them
- [ ] `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` is clean (Linux, 2026-11-05) but CI does not run it: add it to CI
- [ ] Sweep orphan databases left in server data by a crash between create's DB rename and its project-file write
- [ ] `meta.createKey` is lost when a rebuild has no old DB (the key is not in the folder)
- [ ] Rational schema pattern `^(0|-?[1-9]\d*)(/[1-9]\d*)?$` accepts non-canonical `0/5` and `3/1` (the parser rejects them)
- [ ] `Probe` TS type: optional fields vs `null` mismatch
- [ ] RPC envelope check vs generated schema is a vitest test (`envelopes.test.ts`), not a `pnpm gates` gate
- [ ] Typecheck gate: project list hardcoded in `scripts/check-typecheck.mjs`; core-types tests not typechecked
- [ ] fin-node `loadNative` untested
- [ ] No e2e or RPC fixture for `tx.get` set-aside / `TX_SET_ASIDE` (unit tests only; a live server cannot produce `SALVAGED`/`SUPERSEDED` without fake clocks)
- [ ] `BASE_REV_AHEAD` vs `HISTORY_REWOUND` precision for a subscriber whose `fromRev` is beyond a rewound head
- [ ] `tx.commit.generation` and `ops.subscribe.generation` optional in protocol 1 → required in protocol 2
- [ ] Linux NFS: directory attribute cache (`acdirmax`, 60 s by default) exceeds the 10 s ordering-rule wait; NFS is classified `network` but untested. Measure, and use a longer wait or a listing refresh there
- [x] Windows (~4.9 s, not-found cache) and macOS smbfs (1.5–2.4 s): `PROJECT_NOT_FOUND` right after another host creates the project → 12 s retry on network storage, listing refresh first on macOS (7716b8a; only under an existing parent, leftover probes swept: 145d137; spec §3.13 item 14). Linux cifs/NFS take the same retry, unmeasured
- [ ] macOS smbfs: whether the lease probe can read a client-set mtime (no `AT_STATX_FORCE_SYNC`); untested
- [ ] Locked files: no test for the pending-deletion path (first sighting without metadata); a verify right after open shows `SEGMENT_LOCKED`, not `unreadable`, until the store has been open 60 s
- [ ] A segment held open by another SMB client slows compaction to one 60 s retry per file
- [x] iCloud: a first read of a dataless file can fail `ETIMEDOUT` (1 in ~25) → placeholder reads that time out are retried 3 × 1 s (`fsio::hydrating`, b23a522; not the lease's reads or the per-poll project-file reread, d022fc3/145d137); unit-tested via a hook, not re-run on iCloud
- [ ] iCloud conflict-copy names and second-device mid-upload view: need a second device
- [ ] Clean close could write a "released" lease record so other servers need not wait out 45 s of staleness
- [ ] `fin project verify` / `rebuild` / `compact` have no progress or cancellation (only `ENGINE_BUSY {operation, sinceMs}`)
- [ ] `diffs::fill` loads a whole branch's rows; restore tooling for daily backups should mint a new incarnation (backups already do; a hand-copied DB does not)
- [ ] Abandoned compaction manifests are never deleted automatically; orphan packs from a crash before the manifest stay as harmless duplicates; one manifest per branch at a time
- [ ] Conflict copies are re-read on every pass (scanner never caches foreign files); a conflicted branch directory is listed, not entered (its segments are not quarantined)
- [ ] A sync folder with losing segments re-reports the fork on every poll. Partly closed by 4B: `fin project recover --import <dir> --create all` brings a moved-out side back as a `recovered-*` branch; the fork itself still needs the manual move-out (in-band resolution: 4B follow-ups)
- [ ] A copy opened while the original's share is unmounted counts as a move; the original is then refused as the copy
- [x] Rewind rule (d) needs 4B to record branch bases (`meta` `branchBase.<b>`) — 4B Task 10 cascades instead; only hand-made roots keep the `branchBase` rule
- [x] Pack headers record replaced segments' last records (`replaces: [{hash, lastTxHash}]`, exact stand-in rule) — done in Task 13
- [x] finisherd hubs removed with their last subscriber — done in Task 17
- [ ] fin-node untested paths: `lagged` (needs > 1,024 events before registration), close on GC drop
- [ ] Warn (status) on Linux mount options that break the lease's assumptions: cifs `cache=loose`, `actimeo`/`closetimeo` > 1, SMB1 (`vers=1.0`)
- [ ] Lease: a failed in-place write of our own record could leave it torn; the holder then self-fences and the folder needs `takeover --confirm`. Consider rewriting a torn record in the heartbeat when no claim above ours exists, as `take` does after its own write
- [ ] Lease: `create_new`-only fallback for the first `lease.json` (Unix without hard links: FAT/exFAT, macOS smbfs as far as known, NAS shares refusing `link` with `EACCES`) still leaves a short record after a crash. Task 19: macOS smbfs takes that path (no hard links); Linux cifs and Windows publish atomically
- [ ] Lease: a claim `create_new` that landed but reported a transient error leaves our own orphan claim (status names it as this server's); it holds acquisition off for 45 s

- [x] Task 2: scrubbed 4A fixture (`crates/fin-store/tests/fixtures/4a`), generated from 4A code
- [x] Task 3: record kinds with typed links, content-versioned segments (edit-only v1, any 4B kind v2), per-kind reader versions, DB migration 0002 (`branches`, `record_links`)
- [x] Task 4: exact per-track shift maps (`rebase::map`: `StepMap`, `Mapping` with mirrors; ProseMirror recover rule, Decision 16)
- [x] Task 5: per-track, per-entity conflict footprints (`rebase::footprint`, one `footprint` per op module; `Conflict` with reason codes, `Conflicts` → `CONFLICT` with `details.conflicts`)
- [x] Task 6: rebase engine rewrite (`rebase_apply`: map op by op through mirrored inverses, judge unmirrored steps, re-run with full policy; `rebase_tx` in the frame at the base, `state::state_at` in commit), `clip.restore` placeholders. Proptest budget, first measure: `rebase_props` + `shift_map` 0.15 s wall in a debug build on the Linux VM (tests 0.05 s + 0.02 s; budget < 8 s). Review fixes: undo/redo mirrors from `CommittedTx.link` (`steps_from`), `Conflict.details` and re-run blame, `details.baseRev` on a base failure, commit frame by rewind or replay (`state_near_head`); unchanged timing
- [x] Task 7: one write path (`Store::write`: dedupe, read-only, fence, IMMEDIATE txn, in-memory lease re-check, commit; dry runs in a rolled-back txn with no read-only or lease check, so on followers too; IMMEDIATE after review) used by commit; lineage reads from the `branches` index (`branch::lineage`: `state_at`, `state_near_head`, `records_since` filling pending diffs per ancestor, `hash_at`/`tip_hash` with the fork-hash fallback in `refresh`); `state.rs` removed
- [x] Task 8: branch index derived from the log (`fin_core::branch`: `BranchIndex`, name rules, references `id | name | name#id`, duplicate names held by the lowest numeric id); `Store::create_branch` (`branch` record on the parent, index row, fork snapshot), `Store::set_branch` (`branchSet`: rename, archive, restore, `--restore --as`), `Store::branches`, `Store::resolve_branch`; references resolved in commit and reads; `NAME_TAKEN`, `BRANCH_ARCHIVED` (exit 3); `derived::recompute`
- [x] Task 9: branch-aware import (worklist parents first; children learnt from applied `branch` records in the same pass, with their fork snapshot; segments must start at the fork point, `Known::fork`; unfit `branch`/`branchSet` records set aside `MALFORMED`, replaced by inert rows in Task 9b), Decision 8 (a set-aside `branch` record takes its directories' segments along, recursively; the same id made on two servers across a takeover keeps the set-aside branch's segments off the live one; directories with an unknown `branch` record wait: `unknownBranches`), export parents first, counters past branch ids and quarantined ids (`Counters::raise_to_quarantine`), status `branches[]` gains `name, parent, forkSeq, state`; Task 8 review fixes (writes refuse index-less branches, absorb validates names and fork hashes, `try-<n>-2` defaults, no-op `branchSet` refused)
- [x] Task 9b: branch identity across servers (Decision 33): `branch` records carry their fence `epoch`; an id made twice goes to (epoch desc, txHash asc), the loser is an inert row (its branch's content `ID_COLLISION`, applied subtrees voided through the cascade), unfit branch records are inert rows too (`record_links.ignored`, warning `BRANCH_RECORDS_IGNORED`); every branch segment names its `branch` record (`branchRecord`, always format 2) and is classified by it; seeds stop below the first branch record
- [x] Task 10: rewinds and diverged tails cascade to descendant branches whose `branch` record was lost, recursively (`import::cascade`: rows to quarantine by the 4A origin rule, snapshots, `segments` rows and per-branch `meta` deleted, generation bumped from rev 0, a `Rewound` event per branch; interim rule (d) gone, rule (b) counts the cascaded rows); `derived::recompute` after every rewind, diverged tail and cascade (stale `record_links` rows dropped, index rebuilt); cascade entry reusable for a voided `branch` record (`cascade::subtree` + `Cause::voided`)
- [x] Task 11: branch-aware verify, rebuild and compaction: verify resolves each 4B branch from the segments naming its index record at its fork point (`Known::for_branch`), `chain_continuity` starts at `forkSeq + 1` with `forkHash` as first parent, new check 9 `branch_index` (index and `record_links` against `derived::compute`, index rows without state, branches without index row, other segments of a branch directory named by a set-aside or unknown record only); rebuild gives the follower's DB back (branches, renames, archived, branch of branch, collisions); compaction resolves at the fork point, never packs across branch records and abandons a manifest naming another record's segment. ### Follow-ups from 4B
- [ ] In-band fork resolution: a record kind chain resolution honours, so `CHAIN_FORK` needs no manual move-out (Decision 29; today: the manual procedure plus `fin project recover --import`)
- [ ] `project.recover` changed its result from `{manual}` to the `RecoverReport` within protocol 1 (not additive; `manual` is still there): record it in protocol 2
- [ ] Conflict reasons: an undo whose clip is back already (re-created by a copy) is `ID_COLLISION`, where `ALREADY_PRESENT` would say it better (the undo planner does not judge origins)
- [ ] Conflict reasons: a copy whose clip is gone may answer the re-run's `NOT_FOUND` with no blamed record when an excluded inverted source undo lies between a mirrored pair; name the excluded record (`REMOVED`/`DEPENDS_ON_SKIPPED`)
- [ ] There is no `fin log` (or other) command to list txIds and mergeIds; `fin cherry-pick`'s `NOT_FOUND` fix names `fin log`, which does not exist (`fin-core/src/history/pick.rs`). Today ids come from each write's answer and `ops.subscribe`
- [ ] Perf: `undo --to` still reads the whole branch; merge planning is O(candidates × (source + target path)) even with prepared steps (800 + 800: 0.75 s debug); `derived::compute` (verify, full recompute) parses every record (222 ms at 1,500 + 1,500 with 1,500 copies); the recover listing (`Forest::load`) parses every current quarantine row; status counts quarantine through a covering index (7.6 ms at 10,000 rows)
- [ ] `recoverDismissed` is local to a server and lost on a rebuild (dismissed groups come back)
- [ ] `REBUILD_WOULD_LOSE.exportedThrough` reports only `main`'s
- [ ] History commands need a running server, also for `--dry-run` (no offline planning)
- [ ] `crates/fin/src/config.rs` module doc still shows the old flat `tries: {<branchId>: <parentId>}`; the file is per project (`tries: {<projectId>: {…}}`)
- [ ] Squashing archived branches (deferred out of sub-project 4; needs a two-phase delete)
- [ ] `BranchCreated`, `HistoryResult`, `MergeReport`, `RecoverReport` are `Schema.Unknown` in contracts (narrowed to generated types); generate Effect schemas with `ProjectStatus`

Then the `rebase_moves_props` oracle allows the unblamed partner-drift refusal (`keyed4c::partner_drift`, fixed case `partner_of_overwrite_move_refuses_unblamed`): a known limitation, see the follow-ups
- [x] Self relocation's refusal (2026-11-08; spec §4.3, §4.6): a workspace run of `rebase_moves_props` found a stale refusal `INSERT_INSIDE_CLIP` (unblamed) where the fresh edit applies. t1 holds c1, c2, c3; the head ripples c2 to the end, so c3 starts at frame 10; ours slides c3 by −1 and then ripple-moves c4 before c2 (frame 10). Frame 10 maps to 10 (c2's removed range collapses to its start), our rebased slide puts c3's start at 9 without a shift, so the move lands inside c3, which the head only shifted (no change to blame). This is the documented *self relocation* limitation (positions map through shifts only, and a slide records none), not an engine bug; the oracle allowed it in equality (`self_relocates`) but not in refusals. Now `keyed4c::self_drift` (the positional ops after our own slide or overwrite move) allows only the position-inside-an-item codes (`INSERT_INSIDE_CLIP`, `MOVE_INTO_ITSELF`, `OVERWRITE_SPLITS_ITEM`) unblamed; `self_relocates` is defined from it. Seed in `rebase_moves_props.proptest-regressions`, fixed case `slide_then_move_at_moved_anchor_refuses_unblamed`. Sibling search: all fin-core property binaries pass; the rebase ones (`rebase_moves_props`, `props_rebase_4c`, `props_places`, `caption_rebase`, `rebase_props`, `rebase`) at `PROPTEST_CASES=2000`, the first four also at 30,000 (1 min 55 s, debug)
- [x] Overwrite keeps a frame of what it trims (`dfda488`, 2026-11-08; spec §5.3, §4.6): 5A's Task 12 run found `props_ops_4c` `apply_tx_never_panics_and_success_keeps_invariants_4c` failing `shorter than a frame` (`c4`, 1/1001 s): an overwrite `item.insert` at 1/1001 s for 2/5 s on `t2`, whose first item `c4` starts at 0. Times need not lie on the frame grid, and the overwrite carve (`ops::util::carve`, shared by overwrite insert, move and trim) trimmed a partly covered item to any length, below the one-frame minimum every other op keeps (split, caption split, `edge_limits`). An engine bug, not a generator gap. `check_carve` now takes the policy frame (`util::policy_frame`, `None` on replay): a range edge less than a frame inside an item it trims is `INVALID_RANGE` with `details.{item, start, end, from, to, remains, min}`, before anything changes (and before an insert allocates its id); replay keeps what was committed. Fixed cases `ops_modes::overwrite_never_leaves_an_item_shorter_than_a_frame` (insert head and tail, move, trim; one frame left and a whole cover still carve; replay applies) and `props_ops_4c::overwrite_at_a_sub_frame_time_keeps_a_frame_4c` (the shrunk input replayed exactly, its `Index` picks recomputed). The seed was not kept and 400,000 fresh cases did not find it again, so the regressions file has a comment pointing at the fixed case. Sibling search: the 4C property binaries (`props_ops_4c`, `props_rebase_4c`, `props_history_4c`, `rebase_moves_props`, `props_places`, `undo_to_props`, `caption_rebase`, `ops_transitions`) pass at `PROPTEST_CASES=2000`, and all fin-core tests pass. wasm32-wasip1 (wasmtime 50.0.1): `eval_corpus`, `ops_modes` pass; `props_ops_4c::generators_reach_every_op_kind` misses `timeline.restore` there, before this change too (32-bit `usize` changes what the fixed seed generates; not in CI's wasm step). - [ ] **Partner drift** (spec §4.3, §5.11; merge `c42e9ae`): a stale positional op after our own overwrite move of a linked item can be refused by policy (`MOVE_INTO_ITSELF`), unblamed, where a fresh one succeeds, because an overwrite move records no shift and later ops anchored on a partner do not map with it. Fix: overwrite moves carry their partners' positions (a per-partner step), then drop the oracle's `keyed4c::partner_drift` allowance

## Sub-project 5A: principals, local credentials and scopes (implemented 2026-11-08; cross-platform validation pending)

Spec: `docs/specs/2026-11-07-sub-project-5-control-plane-design.md` (revision 6: §3 and §4 as built). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-07-sub-project-5a-principals-and-scopes.md`. Branch `sp5a-principals`, stacked on `sp4c-edit-breadth`. Tasks 1, 2 and 10 needed only D1, D2 and 4C; diagnostics D1–D7 are merged into this branch (2026-11-07), so Tasks 3–9 and 11–14 can proceed. 5C-1 builds on Tasks 1 and 2.

- [x] Task 1: control store (`control.db`, per-area migration chains), environment id, `server_ids`, audit log
- [x] Task 2: key files in `auth/`, the socket in `run/`, private server data on Windows, handshake crypto
- [x] Task 3: connection-aware protocol, principals, the handshake RPCs (not yet enforced). As built: finisherd serves `FinRpcs` through its own NDJSON protocol (`protocol.ts`) instead of Effect's `makeProtocolSocketServer`: it caps inbound lines at 16 MiB (`FRAME_TOO_LARGE` to the request id read from the line's start, then close; the WebSocket's `maxPayload` is the cap plus 64 KiB), answers bad JSON, unknown frames, unknown tags, bad headers and payloads its schema refuses with `INVALID_REQUEST` (decoded here with the codec and parse options `RpcServer` uses, so the server's own decode never fails; a frame with no id to answer closes the connection), drops `x-fin-` headers and registers each connection with a `Link` (close, close after the reply, answer open requests then close). `connections.ts` records Effect 4.0.6's facts (client ids per protocol, so `Connections` is per transport; disconnects; where decoding fails); `ConnectionRegistry` sums the transports. `auth/handshake.ts` (`Handshake`: hello with `envId`, canonical endpoint, `serverNonce` and the proof only for a live credential with matching `env`/`endpoint`; one prove per hello; five failures close after the reply; `auth.fail` audited with `transport`), `auth/gate.ts` (`withAuthentication`: every RPC but `server.hello`/`auth.prove`; refusals counted in `rpc.stats`), `principal.ts`, contracts `auth.ts` (`PrincipalSchema`, `CredentialRefSchema`, the hello and `auth.prove` schemas, `PUBLIC_RPCS`, `defaultScopes`) and `scopes.ts` (`SCOPES`, `ScopeSchema`, `HUMAN_ONLY`; Task 5 adds the rules); `RPC_METHODS` += `auth.prove`; audit details gain `transport`. The WebSocket's connections start as `local` (its bearer token is a file of the user's, like `local.key`) until Task 6
- [x] Task 4: `fin` proves; the server requires proofs; exit code 7. As built: `crates/fin/src/auth.rs` (`discover`: `FIN_CREDENTIAL` → a `{v:1, kind: agent|mcp, id, key, env}` file, else `<server-data>/auth/local.key`; keys decode strictly (43 canonical base64url chars); an unreadable or malformed credential is none; `handshake`: hello `{clientNonce, env?, endpoint, credential}`, checks `envId`, the canonical endpoint and the proof (HMAC-SHA-256, constant time), then `auth.prove {mac}`; `connect` retries once after re-reading `finisherd.json` (unless `FIN_SERVER`) and the key, noting `keyStale` when the key changed; a second failure is `SERVER_UNTRUSTED` `{endpoint, reason: noProof|badProof|envMismatch|endpointMismatch}` with the fix in words; no credential → no handshake, and the server's `UNAUTHENTICATED` gains `reason: noCredential` and a fix naming `FIN_CREDENTIAL` and the server's data; test hook `FIN_TEST_AUTH_GATE`), `fin whoami` → `{principal, retried?}` (`anonymous` without a credential; `cli.command` name `whoami`), exit 7 `DENIED` for `DENIED_CODES` (11 codes); `INVALID_REQUEST` → 3, `FRAME_TOO_LARGE` → 2. `console` is not a fin credential kind (it is `finisherd <command>`'s). finisherd: `ENFORCE` and the legacy `local` principal are removed rather than flipped (an unproved connection is `anonymous`; `auth.legacy` stays a readable audit kind, never written); TS raw-socket tests prove first (`ndjson-client.ts`). `scripts/e2e/lib.mjs` needed no change: e2e talks over the WebSocket, `local` until Task 6
- [x] Task 5: middleware chain, scopes, reach, policy and the write guard. As built: contracts `scopes.ts` gains `FinRpcTag`, `RPC_SCOPES` (every RPC: its scopes, all required; resolvers; escalations; `writes`, the payload fields naming a write target) and `requiredScopes`; `chain/` in finisherd (`chain.ts` `withChain` replaces `auth/gate.ts`, unary and stream alike; `resolve.ts`, `authorize.ts`, `policy.ts`, `fixes.ts`): authenticate → resolve (projects, `openDir` with `finisher.project.json`'s id and whether it is open here, `createDir`, `probePath` and `importDir` canonical; the handler gets the canonical probe path) → authorise (human-only first, then every scope, then reach: `principal.projects` → `PROJECT_NOT_FOUND`, paths outside the principal's open projects' folders → `PATH_NOT_ALLOWED`) → policy (branch write targets resolved through the store, then agent branch limits: `"own"` = branches whose `createdBy` is `<kind>:<id>`; `ask` → `APPROVAL_REQUIRED {approvalId}`, approvals in memory) → handler with `options.guard`/`options.principal` → after (`project.open` must open the project its folder named for principals with project limits). Branch references resolve after authorisation, not before: resolving needs the project's engine, which an unauthorised caller must not get opened, and only the policy reads them. Every denial is audited (`auth.denied {rpc, rule, scope|scopes, reason, transport}`; `UNAUTHENTICATED` denials aggregate per minute like `auth.fail`) and counted in `rpc.stats`. The guard rides in each write's `metaJson` (`{actor, guard}`) to fin-node, which applies it around every store write (`Store::with_guard`); fin-store checks it in `WriteCtx::target`, inside the IMMEDIATE transaction, after resolving the target branch (`BRANCH_NOT_ALLOWED`, `BRANCH_PROTECTED`). `project.takeover {confirm: true}` and `project.open {relocate: true}` are human-only now: their tests use the console (`crates/fin/tests/support/console.rs`; the `project-open-relocate` fixture is captured and replayed as `console`). There are no job or agent RPCs in `FinRpcs` yet (5C-2 and 7A-2 add them): the mapped type makes each new one a type error until it has a rule
- ~~Task 7 (from Task 5)~~ (done in Task 7): the diagnostics RPCs keep `diagnostics.read` in `RPC_SCOPES` until Task 7 makes `diagnostics.consent`, `diagnostics.purge`, `diagnostics.project {collect: "default"}` and content exports need `diagnostics.admin` (an `escalate` on each), with `human_only_matrix` (`apps/finisherd/test/chain.test.ts`) extended to them and their fin and e2e steps moved to the console in the same commit. No `agent`/`mcp` credential exists before Task 9, so nothing is exposed meanwhile
- Task 9 (from Task 5): agent credentials' authors: the chain's `"own"` compares a branch's `createdBy` with `<kind>:<id>` (`chain/policy.ts` `authorOf`); since the Task 5 review the server stamps it on `branch.create` and `project.recover` for branch-limited principals (`RuleOf.author`); other records' `author` fields still come from the payload — **done in Task 9**: credentials carry no author of their own; `createdBy` of a branch they make is `<kind>:<id>` (checked end to end in `credentials.test.ts` `branches own`)
- Review of Task 5 (fixed on `sp5a-t6`, commit `fix(auth): Task 5 review`): H1 every write of a branch-limited principal carries `allowBranches` (rules that write declare `writes`, `[]` allowed; `WRITE_RPCS`), and a write without the chain's guard, or limited without an allow-list, is a defect (`writeGuardOf`, also L3); H2 `project.open`/`create` get the canonical folder, `registry.open` checks the project the chain read before registering it (`PROJECT_NOT_FOUND {reason: changed}`) and only attaches when loading was not checked; M1 serving a project that is not open needs `project.write` (`RuleFacts.reopens`); M2 paths canonicalise through their nearest existing ancestor, and `asset.probe` re-checks the real path around the probe for principals with roots; M3 `finisher.project.json` is opened `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, a regular file of at most 64 KiB; M4 authors stamped (above); M5 `auth.denied` aggregates per `(principal, code, rpc)` per minute and stream refusals are counted in `rpc.stats`; L1 human-only, then the RPC's own scopes, then reach, then the facts' escalations (no open-state oracle); L2 `allowBranches: null` refused, the generated type has no `| null`; L5 escalations typed by their RPC's payload (`RuleOf<T>`); L7 `fin` reads only regular key files, opened non-blocking
- **Before Task 9** (the first `agent`/`mcp` credentials), from the Task 5 review: ~~Task 7 is a hard prerequisite~~ (done); **L4** streams are authorised once, at subscribe: revocation and expiry must end live streams (`Connections.closeWhere` and the stream's own fiber), not only refuse new calls; residuals Task 9 must close or accept: the probe's real path is re-checked around the probe, not on the opened file (an fd-based check in fin-node), and a project evicted between the chain's open-state check and the handler is reopened for a `project.read` caller — **done in Task 9**: **L4** revocation (owner, self) and expiry (the 60 s sweep, and every request re-checks liveness at once) go the one revoke path, which ends the credential's connections and so its streams (`revoke_closes_live_connections`: a live `ops.subscribe` ends `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED` within 100 ms; expiry likewise); the probe residual was closed by the Task 6 review (`probeBeneath`, fd-based); the eviction residual is accepted again below (**9**)
- Sub-project 7 (from the Task 5 review, **L6**): cap `approvals` (the chain's in-memory `ask` map) and expire its entries when approvals become real — **done in Task 9** (cheap enough now): `approvals` holds at most `MAX_APPROVALS` (256), each for `APPROVAL_TTL_MS` (10 min), oldest dropped first (`keepApproval` in `chain/chain.ts`); sub-project 7 may keep real approvals longer in its own store
- [x] Task 6: WebSocket tickets, Host and Origin checks, protocol 2. As built: `PROTOCOL_VERSION = 2` (contracts and `fin`); `tx.commit.generation` and `ops.subscribe.generation` required; `asset.probe {projectId, path}` (resolvers `project` + `probePath`: reach on the project, and for principals with roots the path must be in that project's folder; the handler opens the project on demand, `PROJECT_NOT_FOUND` otherwise; `fin` sends the project it opened); `auth.ticket {ttlS?}` (`server.read`; 30 s default, capped at 60, at least 1; 32 random bytes base64url; only `SHA-256(ticket)` kept with the principal and expiry; one use, an expired ticket is used up by the attempt too; swept every 10 s; at most 1,024 live, the oldest dropped; `auth.ticket` audited), `auth.whoami` and `auth.revokeSelf` (no id: an `agent`/`mcp` principal revokes its own credential through `HandshakeDeps.revoke`, which Task 9 provides, its tickets are dropped and the connection closes after the reply; `local`/`console` get `INVALID_REQUEST`). `upgrade.ts`: our own `node:http` server on `127.0.0.1:0` with `ws` (now a direct, pinned dependency, 8.22.0) in `noServer` mode; checks path `/rpc` (404), `finisher.v2` offered (426 `{"protocol":{"min":2,"max":2}}`), Host in `127.0.0.1|localhost|[::1]:<port>` (403), Origin absent, one of those `http://` origins or in `settings.json` `server.origins` (403), then exactly one `finisher.ticket.<t>` that is live (401); answers `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol: finisher.v2`; `?ticket=` is never read; refusals other than path and version are audited `auth.fail {credentialKind: ticket, reason}` (aggregated). Each accepted socket carries its ticket's principal into `protocol.ts` (`UpgradePrincipal`, `Connections.attach(…, principal)`): the WebSocket no longer starts as `local`. `finisherd.json` has no `token`. `scripts/e2e/lib.mjs` `connectWs(server)` proves over the socket (`socketCall`, the same MACs, checked against the shared vectors in `scripts/e2e.test.mjs`) and mints the ticket. The RPC fixtures are re-captured: writes carry a generation, the incarnation is normalised to `01fixtureincarnation000000` and `rpc_contract.rs` substitutes this run's; request ids restart at 1 after the handshake. `fin`'s `versionMismatch` is now `protocolVersion < 2` alone
- ~~Task 7 (from Task 6)~~ (done in Task 7): the WebSocket's journal source is still D4's per-transport `ui`, so a `local` ticket over the WebSocket can still opt in to training (`diagnostics.consent training on`) until Task 7 takes sources from principals (`sourceOf`) and makes training on device-only. `scripts/e2e/lib.mjs` `socketCall` and `apps/finisherd/test/support/ws-client.ts` are the helpers to extend with the console challenge
- [x] Task 7: diagnostics sources and actors from principals; human-only diagnostics. As built: `fin_core::events::Source` gains `console` and `mcp` (`mcp` emits `cli.command` and `error.panic`, `console` nothing); `RPC_METHODS` += `console.challenge`, `CLI_FLAGS` += `content`. finisherd: D4's per-transport `RpcSource` is gone; `sourceOf(principal)` (`local`/`agent` → `fin`, `mcp` → `mcp`, `console` → `console`, `device` → `ui`) and `actorFor(principal, headers)` (`agent`/`mcp` always `{agent, session: <credential id>}`, only `call` from the header; `device` `{user, session}`; `console` `{user}`; `local` D6's header actor); one run id per connection across transports; the `diagnostics.record` rate limit per principal (`local`, `console`, `<kind>:<id>`, at most 1,024 buckets). `RPC_SCOPES`: `diagnostics.consent` and `diagnostics.purge` need `diagnostics.admin`; `diagnostics.project {collect: default}` and `diagnostics.export {content: true}` escalate to it; rules gain `deviceOnly` (training on → `CONSENT_UI_ONLY` for every principal but a `device`, before the human check, the console included; its fix in words, `where: app`) and `confirm` (`project.open`, `project.takeover`, `diagnostics.consent|purge|project`). The server half of Decision 30: `console.challenge {}` (`auth.admin`, console principals only; `INVALID_REQUEST` otherwise) → a 6-character Crockford code valid 60 s, kept (as SHA-256) on the connection itself, at most 4 live; the chain's last stage refuses a `confirm` call that needs a human-only scope from a `console` principal without its connection's live code (`CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED`, exit 7, audited `console.refused`); typed codes are read the Crockford way (case, I/L/O); a code is used by the call it confirms, a wrong answer voids all of the connection's codes. The payloads of the confirmed RPCs take `challenge`. Exports by a principal that is not a person are redacted (`ExportPage.redact()` in fin-journal: content fields and their blobs removed, `redacted: true`; fin-node `export(…, redact)`). Consent changes and purges are audited (`consent.change {purpose, value}`, `diagnostics.purge {reason: all|project|before}`). `fin diagnostics export --content` sends `content: true` (`HUMAN_ONLY` for `fin`). Test helpers: `confirmed()` (`ndjson-client.ts`), `WsClient.confirmed`, `Console::confirmed`/`confirmed_ok` (`crates/fin/tests/support/console.rs`), `socketCall(…, {confirm})`, `consoleCall` and `storedEntries` (`scripts/e2e/lib.mjs`). The RPC fixtures of the confirmed calls are captured as the console with the code normalised to `0FIXTURECHALLENGE0`, which `rpc_contract.rs` replaces with its own connection's; `diagnostics-consent-human-only` records `fin`'s refusal. README's diagnostics section says which commands are the owner's
- Task 8 (from Task 7): the server half of the confirmations exists; the console CLI calls `console.challenge` on its own connection, shows the code, and sends the RPC with `challenge: <typed>` only after the person types it (a refusal says `reason: missing|wrong|expired`; after `wrong` ask for a new code). `finisherd diagnostics consent training on` already answers `CONSENT_UI_ONLY` from the server; the console may also refuse it locally. Content exports need no code (not a change)
- Review of Task 6 and its Task 5 fixes (fixed on `sp5a-t7`, commit `fix(auth): Task 6 review`): **1** fin-probe opens every probed or fingerprinted file without blocking (`open.rs`: `O_NONBLOCK`, then fstat, anything but a regular file is `ASSET_UNREADABLE {reason: NotAFile}`, then `O_NONBLOCK` cleared), so a FIFO cannot hold a libuv thread; **2** principals with roots probe through `probeBeneath(root, path)` (fin-node; `fin_probe::probe_beneath`): Linux `openat2(RESOLVE_BENEATH|RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS)` with a per-component `openat(O_NOFOLLOW)` walk as fallback (also macOS and other Unix), Windows `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` plus `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW` under the root; a dangling or swapped symlink is `PATH_NOT_ALLOWED` and nothing of its target is read (`stillCanonical` is gone); **3** one revoke path (`auth/revoke.ts` `revokeCredential`: the store, the credential's tickets, and every live connection it proved on every transport, ended with `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED`, which ends its streams; `auth.revokeSelf` uses it and closes its own connection after the reply; Task 9's owner revoke and expiry sweep must call it); **4** tickets: at most 8 live per principal (a newer one drops that principal's oldest), 4 mints/s per principal (burst 16), and past the global 1,024 a mint is refused (`TICKET_LIMIT`, exit 5) instead of evicting another principal's ticket; **5** `project.open` by a principal with project limits answers a folder of another project exactly like a folder with none (`PROJECT_NOT_FOUND`, no id); **6** `Engine.open(…, {expectId})` (`OpenOptions::expect_id`): the store refuses a folder that no longer holds the checked project before it rebuilds, locks or leases anything; **7** rules gain `targets` (branches acted on without a guarded write): `project.acknowledgeRewind` is branch-limited; **8** the WebSocket listener cuts off clients that take over 10 s for their headers (checked every second), holds at most 64 connections, and re-reads `settings.json`'s origins only when the file changes (`cachedOrigins`)
- **9** (accepted residual, review of Task 6): a project evicted between the chain's open-state check and its handler is reopened for a `project.read` caller (the chain saw it open, so no `project.write` was asked for). Reopening takes the lease as any open does; accepted because eviction follows a dead engine, and the caller could have read it a moment earlier — **re-checked in Task 9, still accepted**: an agent or MCP credential reaches only its own projects (reach is checked before the handler), and reopening cannot take a lease another server holds (a takeover needs `project.admin` and the console's confirmation), so a `project.read` credential gains nothing it lacked a moment before; it cannot evict an engine itself except by crashing it, which is a bug of its own
- **10** (for Task 9, review of Task 6): `rootsOf` knows only the open projects of a principal with roots, so `asset.probe` for one of its projects that is not open is `PATH_NOT_ALLOWED` even though the handler would open it. Task 9 should take the folder from the registry index (`app.db`) for the principal's own projects, or open the project first — **done in Task 9**: `rootsOf` takes a project that is not open from the registry index (`ChainProjects.indexedDirOf`, `app.db`); the handler opens it (checking it is still that project) and `probe.ts` probes beneath the open folder
- Review of Task 7 and the Task 6 fixes (fixed on `sp5a-t8`, commit `fix(auth): Task 7 review`): **M1** `local` (every `fin` and tool of the user) has its ticket mint bucket and 8-ticket cap per minting connection (`local:<transport>:<clientId>`), under `MAX_TICKETS_LOCAL` (64) live and `LOCAL_MINT_RATE` (16/s, burst 64) for `local` as a whole, refused rather than evicting; the `diagnostics.record` limiter is per connection for `local` too, under `LOCAL_RECORD_RATE` (200/s, burst 800); **M2** the WebSocket listener caps sockets that have not upgraded on their own (`MAX_UNAUTHENTICATED` 16; one that does not upgrade within the header timeout is closed, also when it sends nothing), and WebSockets per ticket owner (8), for `local` as a whole (32) and in all (64, of which 16 only for `device` and `console`): past a cap the upgrade is 503 `tooMany` (not audited as an auth failure); **M3** `ConnectionRegistry` keeps the credentials revoked while the server runs: a connection registering with one (a ticket taken before the revocation) is closed at once, and every request re-checks (`principalOf` is `anonymous`, the chain answers `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED`, audited `auth.denied {rule: revoked}`); **L2** a call that needs a human-only scope needs the console's typed code by default (`needsConfirmation`); a rule opts out only with `unconfirmed: readOnly | service` (`console.challenge` and `diagnostics.export` today); **L3** `probeBeneath` opens its root `O_NOFOLLOW` (Windows: no reparse point), and `probe.ts` asks `rootsOf` whether a principal is restricted, as the chain does; **L4** exports carry content only with `content: true` (a person's export without it is redacted too); **L5** a redacted export page drops entries of kinds or kind versions this build does not know, as the upload view does. Without a credential store (5A) a revocation is audited (`auth.revokeSelf` leaves a `credential.revoke` row); with Task 9's store, only a credential that was live
- **Task 9 prerequisite** (review of Task 7, M3): a write already handed to fin-node's worker when its credential is revoked still commits. Carry a credential epoch (or the credential id) in the `WriteGuard` and have fin-store re-check liveness inside its write transaction, so that a revoked credential's write in flight is refused; until then the window is one in-flight write per connection — **done in Task 9**: `WriteGuard.credential` (`<kind>:<id>`, every `agent`/`mcp` write; a write that does not name its principal's credential is a defect, `writeGuardOf`); `revokeCredential` calls fin-node `endCredential` before it closes connections, and `Store::write` checks the guard's credential under a process-wide read lock held **through the SQLite commit** (`fin_store::end_credential` takes the write lock), so a write past the check commits before the revocation returns and every later one is `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED` inside its transaction, writing nothing (`crates/fin-store/tests/guard.rs` `ended_credential_writes_nothing`, `end_credential_waits_for_writes_in_flight`). Not covered: `project.acknowledgeRewind` (no store write); expiry ends writes through the sweep (≤ 60 s), while new requests are refused at once

- Accepted residual (review of Task 7, L3): `probeBeneath` sets `RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS` but not `RESOLVE_NO_XDEV`, so a mount point inside a project folder is followed; and a hard link inside the folder to a file outside it is a regular file there and is probed (a path rule cannot tell it apart). Both need the user's own write access inside the project folder — **re-checked in Task 9, still accepted**: no RPC creates a hard link, a mount or a symlink in a project folder (recovery and imports write regular files of their own), so only a process with the user's own file access can make one, and to link or mount a file it must already reach it; an agent credential through `fin` or MCP cannot. Sub-project 7 must keep it so: Landlock without `REFER` or `MAKE_REG` across the session's trees and no mount syscalls under its seccomp filter

- [x] Task 9: agent and MCP credentials. As built: contracts `credentials.ts` (`CredentialSchema`, `CredentialFileSchema` `{v:1, kind, id, key, env}`, `MINT_DEFAULT_SCOPES`, `CREDENTIAL_EXPIRY`) and the RPCs `auth.credential.mint` (`auth.admin`, confirmed), `auth.credential.list` (`auth.admin`, `unconfirmed: readOnly`, `{all?}`) and `auth.credential.revoke {id}` (`auth.admin`, confirmed; `{revoked}`); `RPC_METHODS` += the three. finisherd `auth/credentials.ts` (`Credentials`, chain `auth` 0001 `credentials`, which adds `revokedReason owner|self|expired` beside Decision 32's columns): ids are lowercase ULIDs; keys are `credentialKey(credential.master, kind, id)`, never stored; mint (a person only) defaults scopes to `MINT_DEFAULT_SCOPES`, refuses human-only ones (`SCOPE_DENIED` naming them), takes `projects` (ids, default `"*"`), `branches` (`own` or ids/names, default `"*"`), `idleS`/`absoluteS` (60 s – 366 d; `absoluteS: null` none; defaults agent 24 h/7 d, mcp 30 d/none), a label (1–64, no control characters), at most 4,096 live (`CREDENTIAL_LIMIT`, exit 5); rows load at start (ended over 180 days ago deleted); `lookup` gives the handshake a key and principal only while live; `touch` on every request of the credential, written at most once a minute; `isLive` re-checked on every request through `ConnectionRegistry.useLiveness`, so an expired credential runs nothing more at once and the 60 s sweep (`sweepExpired`) ends its connections and streams; audited `credential.mint {credentialId, scopes}`, `credential.revoke {rpc, reason, count, credentialId}`, `credential.expire {reason, count, credentialId}`. Revocation (owner, self, expiry) is the one path of `auth/revoke.ts`: store, every transport's per-request refusal, fin-store's in-flight writes (`endCredential`), tickets, connections. The chain puts `credential: <kind>:<id>` in every agent/MCP guard and notes each request (`ChainDeps.touch`); `FIN_CREDENTIAL` joins `childEnv`. Console: `agent mint --label <s> [--scope]… [--project]… [--branch own|<id>]… [--idle <d>] [--absolute <d>|none] [--out <file>]` (the file is created `wx` 0600 before the mint and removed if the mint does not happen; without `--out` the file is shown once), `agent ls [--all] [--json]` (no terminal needed), `agent revoke <id>`, `mcp setup --out <file> [--label] [--scope]…`. `fin`: on Windows a `FIN_CREDENTIAL` file's DACL is checked; a `noProof` while `FIN_CREDENTIAL` is set says the credential may be revoked or expired. Tests: `apps/finisherd/test/credentials.test.ts` (units with an injected clock and a server run in-process), `console.test.ts` (the commands), `crates/fin/tests/cli_auth.rs` `agent_credential_proves_and_is_revoked`, `crates/fin-store/tests/guard.rs`

- ~~Task 13 (from Task 9)~~ (done in Task 13): README security model: agent and MCP credentials (mint defaults, expiry, `--branch own`), that a credential file is a bearer secret (0600; on Windows the console's `--out` file has a protected owner-only DACL, and `fin` refuses a file others can read), what revocation stops (spec §4.8 "What a revocation stops": store writes and the three acknowledge/collect calls, not long maintenance calls or other calls already running), that `auth/` must stay out of backups (excluded from Time Machine automatically; elsewhere by hand) and `finisherd auth rotate-master` after a restore or a leak, that unsandboxed harnesses run as the owner (scopes bind only well-behaved ones until sub-project 7), `mcp`/`--absolute none` credentials living while used; spec §4.1 as built: `revokedReason`, `CREDENTIAL_LIMIT`, `auth.credential.list {all}`
- Review of Tasks 8 and 9 (fixed on `sp5a-t9-fix`, commit `fix(auth): Tasks 8-9 review`; `home/sp4c-edit-breadth` merged in first, so 5A Tasks 3–8 and 8A are integrated): **M1** every credential principal (`agent`, `mcp`, 5B's `device`, 8's `plugin`: `policy.ts` `stampsAuthor`) gets `author = authorOf(principal)` on `branch.create` and `project.recover` whatever its branch limits, so an unlimited agent cannot plant an `own` branch for another (`createdBy` is the branch record's `author`, `fin-core` `branch/mod.rs`). **M2** `--out` (`agent mint`, `mcp setup`): fin-node `createPrivateFile(path)` (`private_file.rs`, on `fin-registry`'s now-public `private` module: `O_EXCL` 0600 on Unix, `CREATE_NEW` with a protected owner-only DACL on Windows) checks the open file before the mint (Unix `fstat`: regular, one link, effective uid, no group/other bit; Windows owner and DACL) and removes it on `PRIVATE_FILE_UNSAFE`; `commit` writes all of it and fsyncs; a failed write revokes the credential on the same connection (the server lets the connection that minted a credential revoke it without a second code: `challenges.ts` `noteMinted`/`undoesOwnMint`) and removes the file; a cancelled code, Ctrl-C at the question (`prompt.ts` closes on readline's `SIGINT`) or away from it (`process` `SIGINT`: remove, exit 130) removes it too. **M3** `credentialKey(master, envId, kind, id)` with the label `fin/credential-key/v2` (vectors regenerated, one per environment); `excludeFromBackups(auth/)` at every start (macOS `tmutil addexclusion`, a warning if it fails; Windows and Linux documented); `finisherd auth rotate-master` (`auth.credential.rotateMaster`, `auth.admin`, TTY and typed code): a new master written durably first, then every credential not ended revoked through the one path, audited `credential.rotateMaster {count}`. **L1** `acknowledgeRewind`, `acknowledgeSalvage` and `setCollect` take `metaJson` and hold `fin_store::live_credential` through their change (`withCredential` in `handlers.ts`); the "nothing lands" claim in `revoke.ts` and spec §4.8 now names what it covers and what it does not. **L2** revocation marks the row ended in memory (`markEnded`), does steps 2–5, then `persist`s (retried 1 s → 60 s; `dispose` at shutdown after a last try); `sweepExpired` goes on past a failure. **L3** spec §4.1: streams up to 60 s past expiry; `mcp` and `--absolute none` live while used. **L4** (owner decision) a row that exists but has ended gets the server's proof and `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED`/`CREDENTIAL_EXPIRED` (new, exit 7) on prove (`Credentials.forHello`), unknown ids still no proof; `fin`'s `noProof` fix now says the server does not know the credential; `CREDENTIAL_LIMIT` moves from exit 5 to **4**: the server's state refuses it and waiting changes nothing (only the owner mints, so 7's "needs a different principal" would mislead; revoke some, then retry, which is exit 4's meaning). **L5** `MAX_APPROVALS_PER_PRINCIPAL` (16) under the global 256: a principal's own oldest goes first. **L6** the mint's confirmation says "ALL projects (*): every project on this server, and any folder path…" when no `--project` is given; `rootsOf` keeps an indexed (not open) project's folder as a root only while its `finisher.project.json` still names the project. **L7** spec §4.6: an unsandboxed harness runs as the owner and can read `auth/console.key` or make links; until sub-project 7's sandbox, agent scopes constrain only well-behaved harnesses. **Task 8** `auth.audit.purge` (`Audit.ownerPurge`) never deletes `audit.purge` rows, records the cutoff (`details.before`, a new common audit field) and deletes and records in one transaction (a busy store changes neither); a `service` opt-out (`server.stop`) holds for the console only: any other human principal (5B's devices with `auth.admin`) is `CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED` until 5B gives it a way to confirm. **WebSocket** at the 16 unauthenticated slots a new socket first closes the ones that waited over `AT_CAP_GRACE_MS` (1 s). Tests: `credentials-review.test.ts` (12), `chain.test.ts` (+3), `upgrade-limits.test.ts` (+1), `credentials.test.ts` (L4, L5), `handshake-vectors.test.ts` (env binding), `cli_auth.rs` `agent_credential_proves_and_is_revoked` (revoked → `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED`, unknown → `SERVER_UNTRUSTED`)
  - **Residuals:** (1) restoring an old `control.db` beside the same `credential.master` brings back credentials revoked after that copy; the remedy is `rotate-master` (spec §4.1); (2) a revocation not yet written when the server stops or crashes (a store busy past its retries) brings the credential back at the next start; (3) the 16 unauthenticated WebSocket slots are not reserved for anyone: a local user reopening sockets faster than the 1 s grace still blocks new app WebSockets meanwhile; (4) calls outside the covered paths (spec §4.8) that were already past the chain's check may finish after a revocation; (5) the revocation's wait for writes in flight runs on the main thread: revoking during a long commit stalls the server until it lands; (6) **5B must** make `server.stop` from a device with `auth.admin` confirmable (the chain refuses it now); (7) a Ctrl-C while the mint request is in flight can leave a live credential whose file was removed (no one has its key; `agent ls` shows it, `agent revoke` ends it); (8) on Windows the console's credential file is created with the protected DACL by `fin-registry`'s `create_new` (not run here: no Windows host in this review)
- [x] Task 10: RPC hardening (`INVALID_PATH`, `fin` frame cap and deadline, `fin` test hooks behind a feature)
- [x] Task 11: native Windows pipe listener. As built: `crates/fin-node/src/{pipe_win, pipe_loop, pipe_io, pipe_sec}.rs` (napi-free, Windows only) and `pipe_server.rs` (napi `PipeServer`; `PIPE_UNSUPPORTED` elsewhere). One thread owns every instance and one I/O completion port: overlapped `ConnectNamedPipe`, one pulled read (≤ 64 KiB) per connection, writes, and posted commands. The first instance (`FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`, else `PIPE_SQUATTED`, exit 1) is an *anchor*: disconnected at once and kept until shutdown, it takes no client, keeps the name held while every other instance closes, and holds `WRITE_OWNER`, which Windows refuses to every later instance (found on the host) and `allow` needs. Instances: byte mode, `PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS`, `D:P(A;;GA;;;<user>)(A;;GA;;;SY)`; at most 64 client instances (`maxConnections`, the anchor is one more). Deviations from Decision 38: an allowed SID gets `0x12008b` (`FILE_GENERIC_READ | FILE_WRITE_DATA`), not `GRGW`, because `FILE_APPEND_DATA` is `FILE_CREATE_PIPE_INSTANCE` and would let a sandbox host the pipe; once any SID is allowed the descriptor carries `S:(ML;;NW;;;LW)` (an AppContainer runs at low integrity), and the peer check refuses every client that is not this user (or an allowed SID), and every low-integrity one that is not an allowed container. A client is checked from `GetNamedPipeClientProcessId` and its token (user, integrity, AppContainer) before its first byte; a refused one is disconnected and audited `auth.fail {credentialKind: pipe, reason: peer|unidentified, peer}`. Close is graceful (writes finish, then the handle closes once `FilePipeLocalInformation` shows the client read everything, it left, or 2 s passed), so `Link.terminate` reaches pipe clients. `apps/finisherd/src/pipe.ts`: each connection is a `PipeStream` (`Duplex`: pulled reads, `_writev` batching) that `NodeSocket.fromDuplex` makes a `Socket`, so the protocol (frame cap before decode, `INVALID_REQUEST`, `Link`) is the socket's; `ConnectionPeer` carries `{pid, sid}` into `Connections.peer` and the handshake's `auth.fail` rows (contracts: `peer` joins `address` in the auth-failure and pairing details). `transport.ts` `makeLocalServer` (Windows: the pipe; elsewhere the Unix socket); `claimSocketPath` does nothing on Windows. `fin` opens the pipe itself (`crates/fin/src/pipe_client.rs`): `GENERIC_READ | FILE_WRITE_DATA` with `SECURITY_SQOS_PRESENT | SECURITY_IDENTIFICATION` (`interprocess` connected at the impersonation level, so a squatter could act as the user), waiting on `ERROR_PIPE_BUSY`. Tests: `crates/fin-node/tests/pipe_server.rs` (Windows: squatting, `FILE_PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS` read back, DACL read back, a deny-only-user token refused, peers, graceful close of 1 MiB, cap, shutdown, a real AppContainer refused, then allowed); `apps/finisherd/test/pipe.test.ts` (a fake native listener on every platform; the real one and a real finisherd on Windows)
- [x] Task 12: e2e `auth` scenario. As built: `scripts/e2e/auth.mjs` (365 lines) and `auth-lib.mjs`; `lib.mjs` gains `rawConnect(server)` (an unproved NDJSON connection; a call answers its Exit, `{_tag: "Closed"}` when the connection ends; on Windows it waits out `ERROR_PIPE_BUSY` (`EBUSY`/`EAGAIN`) for 5 s), `proveConnection(server, credential, {keyFile})` (`local`, `console` or a credential file's `{kind, id, key}`), `consoleRpc(server, tag, payload, {confirm = true})` (proved as `console`; each mutation fetches a fresh `console.challenge` on its own connection; `{confirm: false}` for reads; `consoleCall` is the same thing), `errorCode`, and the `observed`/`outputs` collections the leak grep reads (every key, nonce, proof, MAC, ticket and challenge code the harness handled; every `fin` and console output). Steps: (1) an unproved connection: `snapshot.get` is `UNAUTHENTICATED`, `server.hello` answers, five zero MACs close it, `run/` holds only `fin.sock`; (2) `fin` as `local`: create, asset, insert, marker, undo, `whoami`; (3) `fin diagnostics purge --all`, `consent diagnostics off` and `project takeover --confirm` are `HUMAN_ONLY` (exit 7, no `fix` with a command line), `finisherd diagnostics consent training on` exits 2 `CONSENT_UI_ONLY`, a console purge without a terminal exits 7 `CONSOLE_NEEDS_TTY`, `consoleRpc` purges `{before}` with its typed code, `finisherd audit --json` lists the denials; (4) a ticketed WebSocket streams the revs in order, a reused ticket and a ticket in the query are 401, a foreign Host, another port and a foreign Origin are 403 and spend nothing (the same ticket then upgrades), another protocol is 426 `{"protocol":{"min":2,"max":2}}`; (5) a `project.read` credential with `branch own`: `fin whoami` says `agent`, `view` works, an edit is `SCOPE_DENIED`, a purge or consent change is `HUMAN_ONLY` (human-only comes before scopes); a writer with `branch own`: an edit on `main` (implicit or named) is `BRANCH_NOT_ALLOWED` and writes nothing, `branch new` and an edit on it work, its `log.commit` entries carry its credential id as `actor.session` (the journal keys it: ids that are not upper-case ULIDs, which credential ids are not, so the scenario reads `events/key` through `keyer`), `TICKET_LIMIT` at the 17th ticket of one connection (another connection still mints), revoking the writer ends its live WebSocket stream (`CREDENTIAL_REVOKED`), voids its ticket (401), makes its next `fin` exit 7 `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED`, and a second revoke is `revoked: false`; (6) a stale `finisherd.json` naming a squatter's socket: `fin` exits 7 `SERVER_UNTRUSTED` after its retry and the squatter saw only `server.hello`; a restart (the expiry moved into the past in `control.db` while the server is stopped, `expireCredentialOffline`, because the shortest expiry is 60 s): 100 back-to-back `fin whoami` across it are `ok` or `SERVER_UNAVAILABLE`, never untrusted or unauthenticated, the old `local.key` neither proves over the socket nor satisfies `fin` (`SERVER_UNTRUSTED`), a live credential survives, the revoked one stays `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED`, the expired one is `CREDENTIAL_EXPIRED`; (7) audit rows (`credential.mint` ×3 without labels, `credential.revoke`, `auth.denied` for `HUMAN_ONLY`, `SCOPE_DENIED` and `BRANCH_NOT_ALLOWED`, `auth.fail`, `auth.ticket`, `diagnostics.purge`), and the leak grep: the keys of `auth/` (and the old `local.key`), the three credential keys and everything `observed` over `finisherd.json`, `events/` (blobs included), `control.db` and its WAL, the audit and journal exports, the servers' stderr and every `fin` and console output: no hit (a needle under 16 characters, the challenge codes, must stand alone as a word); a final `purge --all` empties the journal. **Deviations:** the retry after a restart is exercised by running `fin` through the restart, but the run never lands in the retry window (`retried` count 0 in 100 runs): a deterministic proof needs the test hook, which the release `fin` ignores, so it stays `restart_between_read_and_connect_is_retried` in `cli_auth.rs`; the squatter is a Unix socket (Windows: Task 14's pipe squat); a read-only or write credential cannot reach `auth.ticket` without `server.read`; an agent's `diagnostics.record` needs `diagnostics.record`, so the writer's journal holds `log.commit` entries and no `cli.command`. Windows (not run here): the scenario sets TEMP and TMP to an owner-only folder (`icacls /inheritance:r`), raw clients wait out `ERROR_PIPE_BUSY`, `peer.sid` in audit rows is not a needle. - [x] Task 4: Unix host death (watchdog), cgroups, memory limits, foreign processes, abandonment. As built: `fin-workers watchdog <host pid>` (`watchdog.rs`, 195 lines; libc is a plain Unix dependency again): `+g`/`-g`/`+c`/`-c` lines; host exit seen with `pidfd_open` + `poll` (Linux) or `kqueue` `EVFILT_PROC`/`NOTE_EXIT` (macOS), plus a `getppid` check each second; then a non-blocking drain of stdin (what the dead host wrote is in the pipe, so a late `-g` counts), `kill(-pgid, SIGKILL)` and `cgroup.kill` ← `1`, exit 0; it ignores `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`/`SIGHUP`/`SIGPIPE` and then writes `ready` on stdout, which the host waits for (a signal right after `exec` killed it 3 runs in 20 before the handshake); started in its own process group, empty environment, cwd `/`, stdin/stdout pipes and stderr only (Linux `close_range` in `pre_exec`, macOS `posix_spawn` with `CLOEXEC_DEFAULT`), never with PDEATHSIG; the host side (`unix/watchdog.rs`) keeps the list and writes each change under one lock; a monitor thread reaps it, logs, restarts (first at once, then at most once a minute) and re-sends the list; dropping it closes its stdin and waits ≤ 2 s for it to exit; `caps().watchdog`; process groups write `+g` at the group's first `placed` and `-g` at `close_group` (before the leader reap: `minus_g_precedes_the_leader_reap`). Cgroups (`linux/cgroup.rs`, 400 lines): `detect()` needs `0::<path>` on cgroup v2 (mount point from `/proc/self/mounts`), `cgroup.procs` = only us (or, for a second host in the process, `<cg>/host` with us and our children and an empty `<cg>`), `cgroup.kill`, write access to the dir, `cgroup.procs` and `cgroup.subtree_control`; else a logged reason and process groups; `FIN_CGROUPS=off` or `CgroupMode::Off` skip it; setup moves the host into `<cg>/host`, enables the available `+memory +pids` on `<cg>`, `<cg>/jobs` and each group; groups are `jobs/g<n>` (`<n>` unique per process) with `memory.max`, `memory.swap.max` 0 (else the limit is passed by swapping) and `memory.oom.group` 1; leaves `g<n>/p<k>`, the child writes `0` to the leaf's `cgroup.procs` in `pre_exec`; `pids.max` counts threads, so `active_processes: n` becomes `n × 256` tasks (a fork-storm stop, not an exact process count; deviation); OOM = `memory.events` `oom_kill > 0`, peak = `memory.peak`; the first start in a process sweeps leftover `jobs/g*` (kill, remove deepest first). Memory: `RssAnon + RssShmem` (Linux; a file on tmpfs, where this repo's test runner keeps `TMPDIR`, counts as shmem, correctly) and `ri_phys_footprint` (macOS); with process groups a sampler on the timer thread checks each limited group every `rss_sample_ms` and kills it over the limit, every member `Oom` (`for_others` keeps `Oom`), the highest sample as `memory_peak`; `MemoryRule {fixed_mib, ram_fraction, cap_mib}::resolve(total)` in Rust (exported for the engine through napi). Foreign processes: scanned at each `result`, before each kill and at each exit (`/proc/*/stat` pgrp, `proc_listpids(PROC_PGRP_ONLY)`, or the group's `cgroup.procs` tree) into a per-group seen set, so a grandchild killed with its group still counts. Abandonment: every kill (not only `kill_group`) starts a kill wait; a process the kill should have ended that is still alive after `kill_wait_ms` is reported once as `Abandoned`, released from the tables (`GroupEmpty`, callbacks) and logged when it finally exits; test hook `waiter_delay_for_test`. Test hooks `Host::{watchdog_pid_for_test, watchdog_line_for_test, watchdog_groups_for_test}`. `test.oom` gained `mapFile` and `readyFile`; `test.echo`'s `cgroup` report is read by the first statement of `main`. Tests: `host_death_unix.rs` (12; the host is this test binary re-executed and `SIGKILL`ed), `host_cgroup.rs` (10; each test re-runs itself in a `systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes --wait --pipe` unit and is skipped with a notice without one), `host_memory.rs` (4 on Linux). The kill tests (death, cgroup, memory and `host_unix`'s cancel and close tests) passed 20 runs in a row on Linux. Documented gap (Decision 18): with process groups a `setsid` grandchild survives cancel and host death (`setsid_grandchild_escape_is_documented` asserts it survives); cgroup mode kills it (`cgroup_kill_takes_setsid_grandchild`, `setsid_grandchild_gone_after_host_sigkill`). Not run here: macOS (compiled and linted for `aarch64-apple-darwin`)

As built: `crates/fin-node/src/jobhost/{mod, events, napi_types, params}.rs` (mod 320 lines); values cross as JSON strings (the binding's convention), parsed on the JS thread by `packages/fin-node/jobhost.js` (new: `index.js` was at 585 lines, so the loader and error adapter moved to `native.js` and `index.js` re-exports); typings in `index.d.ts` (`JobHostOptions`, `JobHostEvent`, `JobHostExitInfo`, `JobSpawnRequest`, `JobHostCaps`; `Hello`, `HostMsg`, `WorkerMsg` from `@finisher/core-types`). **Deviations:** `JobHost.start` returns `Promise<JobHost>` (`Host::start` waits for the watchdog's `ready` on Unix and makes the spawn broker on Windows, so it runs on a `jobhost-start` thread: Decision 11a); the event queue is bounded by our own in-flight count (a reader waits on a condvar, then a non-blocking call into an unref'd, unbounded threadsafe function) instead of napi's blocking mode, which blocks inside napi-rs's abort read lock that the JS thread takes at teardown; `send` is `false` for a full queue **and** a gone process, and throws `INVALID_REQUEST` for a message that is not a `HostMsg` or too long; `abandonForTest(proc, holdMs = 60000)`; `spawn_and_kill_are_async` bounds every synchronous call and every event callback at 5 ms rather than a timer-lag monitor (a timer's lag on a loaded VM measures the machine). Events: per process `hello`, messages, `stderr`, `exit`; `groupEmpty` after the group's last exit; progress coalesced in `events.rs` (the newest kept, at most every 250 ms, a trailing one handed over by the `jobhost-events` flusher, any other event of the process flushes it first); `exit.info` is `{reason, kill? | what? | expected?/got?, code?, signal?, foreign?, memoryPeak?, stderrTail, lifetimeMs}`; **a process's events can reach JS before its `spawn` promise settles** (separate queues). The host is dropped on a `jobhost-drop` thread (its drop joins the spawn thread, which may be handing an event to the JS thread). Errors: `HostError` → its `code()` with `details.reason` (the named reason, else `unknownGroup`/`groupClosed`/`shutDown`/`program`/`spawner`/`spawn`) and `details.error`; unreadable arguments → `INVALID_REQUEST {field}`. Also: `validateJobParams` (static `PARAMS: &[&[KindParams]]`, `fin_jobs::test_kinds::PARAMS` under `test-hooks`; unknown kind or version → `{ok: false}`), `jobKindVersions`, `resolveMemoryRule(rule, totalBytes)` (Task 4's `MemoryRule::resolve`), `workersBinaryPath(dir?)` (pure `workersFileName`/`workersBinaryPath(dir, platform)` in `loader.js`). Test hooks: `fin-jobhost` gains `Host::kill_proc_for_test` (`SIGKILL` / `TerminateProcess` 137, reported `exited`) and `Host::abandon_for_test` (the waiter holds the observed exit, so the kill wait abandons it); `pauseEventsForTest` sleeps the handing-over threads. Codes (Decision 41) in `crates/fin/src/output.rs`: 3 `INVALID_REQUEST` (the same line and comment as 5A Task 3's, so the merge is clean), `JOB_KIND_UNKNOWN`, `JOB_PARAMS_INVALID`, `JOB_NOT_FOUND`; 4 a new `JOB_RESULT_CODES` list; 5 `JOB_QUEUE_FULL`, `WORKER_UNAVAILABLE`, `WORKER_PROTOCOL_MISMATCH`, `WAIT_TIMEOUT`, `RESOURCE_STUCK`, `WORKER_OOM`; `UNKNOWN_MESSAGE`/`CALL_LIMIT` stay internal (6); `JOBHOST_UNSUPPORTED` not added (Task 5 landed, nothing returns it). The contracts had no code list: `JOB_ERROR_CODES` (code → exit class) in `jobs.ts`, `jobs.test.ts` reads `output.rs`'s lists and checks it. `test_kinds_match_rust_params` no longer skips. Tests: `packages/fin-node/test/jobhost.test.ts` (15: the plan's 12 plus `test_hooks_kill_one_and_abandon`, `bad_arguments_are_invalid_request`, `resolve_memory_rule_matches_rust`), 3 runs in a row green on Linux; mutation `PROGRESS_EVERY` = 0 fails `progress_is_coalesced` and `slow_js_backpressures_the_worker`. **Review fixes:** the event queue is bounded by count and by summed JSON bytes (`eventQueueBytes`, 16 MiB; one larger event goes alone); `spawned`/`exit`/`groupEmpty` and spawn answers are not counted, so the spawn thread does not stall behind a slow JS thread (what remains is in Decision 11a); `fin-jobhost` sends `HostEvent::Spawned{proc, group}` and runs a spawn's callback before it and before the readers start, and `spawn` resolves through the event queue (`_spawn`, consumed by `jobhost.js`), so no event of a process precedes its `spawn` resolution; every promise is a `Settle` guard (`INTERNAL {reason: "unanswered"}` on drop; `failForTest: startThread | hostStart | spawn`, with `HostOptions::panic_on_{start,spawn}_for_test`); params over 512 KiB are `{ok: false}`, `version` is an `f64` checked as a `u32`; the JSON shapes are `fin_jobhost::wire` types generated into core-types (`JobHostOptions`, `JobGroupSpec`, `JobSpawnRequest`, `JobWatch`, `JobMemoryRule`, `JobHostKillReason`, `JobHostEvent`, `JobHostExitInfo`, `JobHostCaps`; `caps()` is JSON now); `send` answers `"ok" | "full" | "gone"`; `UnknownGroup`/`GroupClosed`/`ShutDown` are `JOBHOST_STATE` (3), `HostError::Unsupported` is gone; the contracts parity test checks both directions; `ProcState::reaped` stops `kill_proc_for_test` (now `-> bool`) signalling a reaped pid; `shutdown(graceMs)` rejects bad arguments; `test.progressFlood` takes `stderrBytes`. Tests: jobhost.test.ts 22 (5 runs in a row green); the `eventQueue: 8` and `eventQueueBytes` floods fail with the count or byte cap at `usize::MAX`; `kill_by_pid_skips_a_reaped_process` fails without the `reaped` check; timing bounds are an event-loop p99, hand-over stamps (`handedAtMsForTest`) and `FIN_STRICT_TIMING=1` for the absolute ones. - [x] Task 9a: engine: submit, cold runs, files, host calls, hooks. As built: `apps/finisherd/src/jobs/{engine, runner, host, files, layer, view, calls}.ts` (engine 499 lines, runner 479). `JobEngine` as frozen; `JobEngineTag` is a `Context.Service` in `engine.ts`; **`layerJobEngine`, `buildJobEngine` (the engine as an Effect, the caller closes it) and `JobEngineOptions` live in `layer.ts`** (it imports `engine.ts`; `engine.ts` re-exporting them would be a cycle). `JobEngineOptions` adds `watchdog`, `diagnostics: () => boolean` (`start.diagnostics`, Decision 7; default off until D4), `queueLimit` (50,000) and `testKinds: {overrides, families}` (fields of test kinds and families replaced by name, for the engine's tests; `registerTestKinds` takes the same); `clock` defaults to the layer's `Clock`, and every Effect the engine runs (hooks, calls, logs) runs in the layer's context with that clock. `WorkersView` = `{pools, warm: [], processes, stuck, caps, diagDropped}`. Submit as Decision 27, plus: the decoded params are stored; a kind whose `files` is a function has them validated at submit (`JOB_PARAMS_INVALID {errors}`); **a resource pool that is not defined refuses the submit (`WORKER_UNAVAILABLE {pool}`, no row)**; the job is inserted, indexed and dispatched synchronously, so `submit` resolves with the job `running` when a slot is free. `host.ts` (`EngineHost`) routes events by proc id and holds those of an unclaimed proc until `spawn` claims it, replaying them inside `spawn` with the proc id on each event (a `hello` held before `spawn` resolved would otherwise find no proc and never get its `start`); `groupEmpty` by group; written against the Task 6 review's API too (`send` → `"ok" | "full" | "gone"`, booleans read as ok/gone; `spawned` events ignored; `JobHostExitInfo`/`JobSpawnRequest` derived from `JobHostEvent`/`JobHost.spawn`); `exitVerdict(info, answered)` maps the host's reasons to `worker.exit`'s (`timedOut` → `killed`, `noHello` → `spawnFailed`, `protocolMismatch` → `protocolError`, `killed:{cancel, recycle, idleExpired, hung}` → their own) and the job codes (resource faults are `WORKER_CRASHED {resourceFault: true}` for Task 10; crashes carry `exitCode`/`signal`/`stderrTail` in control.db only). Runner: dir and files → `prepare` when the directory is new (once per directory: retries reuse it, so `extra` stays the first attempt's) → `checkPrepared` → group (`memoryRule`: the kind's, else the family's class rule, resolved with fin-node's `resolveMemoryRule`; QoS `normal` for interactive) → spawn (`childEnv()` + `TMPDIR/TEMP/TMP` = the job's `tmp/`, cwd the job dir, grants the dir and each file) → at `hello`, the dispatched `{kind, version}` must be in `hello.kinds`, else `WORKER_PROTOCOL_MISMATCH {expected, got}` (never retried) → `start` and `watch` (stall `stallS`, timeout `timeoutS`) → after `result`/`error` the watch is cleared and a worker that has not exited 2 s later is killed with its group → at exit: open calls interrupted, `worker.exit`, the group killed (`jobEnded`, leftovers too) and closed, items and checkpoint flushed, then the outcome. Items: one transaction per second or 256 rows; checkpoints: the newest, at most one write per 10 s, flushed at the attempt's end; progress in memory, `events()` at most every 250 ms per job (trailing timer), cleared at the end. Hooks: `enterHooks` (result + `phase: hooks`) after the whole group is gone, then `onSucceeded` with `hookTimeoutS` (60 s → `JOB_TIMEOUT {hook}`), its value replacing the result; then the terminal transition, the directory deleted, `job.done`, the bus, `onDone` listeners (a throw is logged), waiters, and the next dispatch. Test-hooks fin-node (`testHooks` defined): a non-`test` family's success with `foreign > 0` fails `WORKER_CRASHED {foreign}`. **Interim policies (later tasks replace them):** a failure ends the job unless it is a worker `error` with `retry` `backoff`/`fresh` and attempts are left, which re-queues it at once (Task 10); `cancel` ends a queued job `cancelled`, returns a job in its hook phase unchanged (it ends `succeeded`) and answers a running one `WORKER_UNAVAILABLE {notYet: cancel}` (Task 9b); `pause`/`resume` `{notYet}`; `warm(family)` does nothing and warm kinds run cold (Task 11); at start the jobs root is swept except `phase: hooks` jobs' directories and queued rows are indexed, but `running` rows are only logged (Task 9b's restart) and retention does not run yet (9b); `close` stops dispatch, shuts the host down (2 s grace) and waits ≤ 5 s for runners, which then write nothing (rows stay `running` for 9b's recovery). a running one: `cancel {reason: cancel}` to a started worker, the group killed after 2 s (500 ms interactive), or killed at once before the worker's `start` (helpers starting, hello pending); `cancel` resolves once the run ended; a job in its hook phase is returned unchanged. `pause {job}`: queued → `paused` (`pausedFrom: queued`, keeps `createdAt`); a running resumable job (a `resumable` kind, or a batch kind with items left) gets `cancel {reason: pause}` and 10 s, then `paused` (`pausedFrom: running`; **the stopped run is not counted: `attempts` goes back by one**); a running non-resumable job finishes. `pause {all}` stops dispatch and pauses the running resumable jobs; `resume {job}` re-queues a paused job (a `{all}` pause still holds dispatch); `resume {all}` re-queues every paused job and restarts dispatch. As built: `apps/finisherd/src/jobs/isolation.ts` (pure: `classify`, `nextStep`, `backoffMs`, `isResourceFault`, the isolation state machine `startIsolation`/`afterRun`/`nextRun`/`budget`/`withoutDone`) and `retry.ts` (engine side: `runPlan`, `afterSuccess`, `afterFailure`, the `jobs.isolation` memo `{fresh?, isolation?, results?}`). Classes in Decision 31's order: resource (`SIGBUS`, i.e. an `exited` with `signal: bus`, Windows exit code `0xC0000006`, the host's `resourceFault`, an abandoned process, a crash with `resourceHealthy` false), crash (`WORKER_CRASHED` exits, protocol errors), `oom`/`oomWarm`, transient (hung, stalled, timeout, unavailable, helper, spawn, worker `backoff`), `fresh`, deterministic (worker `never`, tampered, foreign, a file `prepare` left wrong), mismatch, hook (`prepare`), cancel. Backoff `5 s × 2ⁿ` (n = attempts made), ≤ 5 min, `nextRunAt` persisted; `fresh`/`oomWarm` once at once; interactive retries at once and is never isolated. Isolation (cold batch jobs, non-interactive): runs re-queued at once with `attempts` kept (no attempt spent), state `{n, pending, inFlightAlone?, queue, poisoned, crashRuns}`; **in-flight items are a set** (several are halved, one runs alone, then the rest); a run of one item that crashes poisons it (`POISON_ITEM {item, cause}`, written to `job_items`, the rest go on); one item left of a longer run runs alone next; no item in flight halves the remainder; budget `min(2n, 2⌈log₂ n⌉ + 2p)`, past it `WORKER_CRASHED {isolation: budget, crashRuns, poisoned}`; `resourceHealthy` is asked before isolating (false or failing → a resource fault). **Every batch kind's success result is `combine(results, items)`** (default `{items: {<item>: value}, itemErrors}`), also for a single run. Tests: `isolation.test.ts` (8: the classification and step tables, backoff, three fast-check models), `retries.test.ts` (21; a pause asked during a run the policy re-queues still pauses the job), `review9b.test.ts` (23), earlier suites updated for backoff (`waitThroughBackoff` in `test/support/jobs.ts`). a bad read changes nothing), `consent()`; `state.json` `{consent, ...other}` written by the writer before the first line it covers, rev = stored rev if the same decision, else stored + 1 (never below the caller's); an entry whose `ctx.project` is not `default` now is not written (not counted as a drop); control messages carry the count of data messages queued before them and the writer catches up exactly that far (a project set to `default` no longer drops the entry recorded right after it), and the batch loop reads the next control message ahead and handles it before any data queued after it; `blobs.rs`: `attach_blob` hashes with the full keyed BLAKE3, queues the bytes (they count against the byte bound), the writer holds them ≤ 10 s / 8 MiB until an entry with training references them, writes them temp-then-rename before the line, counts references per file (rebuilt at open from the files, only when `blobs/` holds any), pins them across the rotation in the same write, and collects zero-count blobs at rotation, cap passes, purge and scan; a reference whose bytes never arrived takes the entry's content with it; a file's referenced blob bytes count towards its rotation so `maxMiB` (files + blobs) can remove them; `purge.rs`: `PurgeSelector {all, project, before(ms)}` → `PurgeReport {removed, files, blobs}`, on the writer (closes the active file; rewrites temp-then-rename; `all` also removes `key`, `state.json`, `blobs/` and the empty dir, and the caller swaps in a new key); the caps moved here and count blobs; `export.rs`: `export(filter, as_upload: Option<Purposes>) -> Export` (closed files only;
