Deviation (fin-journal, Decision 18): data no longer wakes the writer. The writer parks (`park_timeout`) until its flush timer (≤ the flush interval) unless 64 KiB are in flight or the channel is full, and every control message unparks it, because a `try_send` that wakes a sleeping receiver costs 13 µs p50 on the Linux VM, which put diagnostics at +19–27 µs a commit. Cost (`commit_overhead`, release, Linux VM, 1k-item project, ripple trims, one commit per mode per round, 2,999 rounds; the gate is the median paired difference against `off` in the same round, since two p50s differ by up to ±34 µs with nothing changed; five runs): off p50 2,164–2,266 µs; `off-2` (noise floor) −2.0 to +0.6 µs; diagnostics +3.2–7.3 µs (+0.14–0.33 %; ≤ 2 % and ≤ 20 µs); training +5.1–10.6 µs (+0.23–0.47 %; ≤ 5 % and ≤ 50 µs); stalled writer +2.1–7.3 µs; the logging work alone p50 1.66–1.73 µs diagnostics, 2.26–2.39 µs training. `journal_cost` after the change: gate 0.41/0.55 ns, `record` p99 1.07/0.68 µs, writer 616k/s and 374 MiB/s, idle `record_sync` p50 52 µs, stalled 250.1 ms/1.000 s. Not covered in fin-node: a deduped recover create (it needs records set aside by a diverged folder): fin-store `write_outcome.rs` checks the note for it

## Owner decisions of 2026-11-07 (sub-projects 5–8, cross-cutting)

Recorded in each sub-project spec's "Owner decisions (2026-11-07)" section and in the architecture spec (D7, D8, D13, D14, D28, D30, D31, D34, D35, D36; §8, §12.1, §14–§16, §18, §21, §21.1).

- [x] Sub-project 5 (spec §12): all six as recommended (review state in `control.db`; human-only from 5A through the TTY console; browsers on trusted certificates or an SSH tunnel; audit log keeps device names and IPs for pairing, revocation and failed authentication only, local, 180 days, never uploaded; `local` keeps `branch.protected`/`review.decide`; human-only advisory against raw-shell agents until 7C, documented)
- [x] Sub-project 6 (spec §21), sub-project 7 (spec §20), sub-project 8 (spec §20): recorded; the designs and first-batch plans follow them
- [x] Platform changes (D36, D31): Windows 11 only; Linux kernel ≥ 6.2
- [x] **Console confirmation UX (owner decision, 2026-11-08; spec sub-project 5 §4.7, §12):** the typed 6-character code is gone from the person's side. Most human-only console actions ask `Proceed? [y/N]` in a real TTY; irreversible or destructive ones ask for the target's name typed back (`audit purge`: `audit`; `diagnostics purge`: `diagnostics`; `auth rotate-master`: `credential.master`; `library rm` and `library set --accept-unmounted|--accept-empty-root`: the library's name; `registry rm`: its id; `registry forget`: the root's first 8 characters; `plugin rm --purge-data` and a third-party code install: the plugin's id; `project takeover --confirm`: the project id's first 8 characters; `project open --relocate`: the folder's name; 7A's `agent delete`: the session id's first 8 characters; `provider key rm`: the key id's first 8 characters; `provider local rm`: the word `local`; `agent approve`, `provider key set`, `provider local set` and `training closed-targets` ask y/N). `CONSOLE_NEEDS_TTY` stays; the server still requires a fresh `console.challenge` code, which the console fetches and sends itself after the person confirms (one silent retry on `wrong`/`expired`). Built on `console-confirm` (`confirm.ts` `proceed`/`typeName`/`ask`/`withChallenge`; tests `console-confirm.test.ts`, `support/person.ts`, `console.test.ts`, `console-plugin.test.ts`, `credentials-review.test.ts`, `agent-console.test.ts`; README security model). Follow-ups: 5B's console commands (`tls rotate`, `env reset` name-typed), 5E's `review policy set` (y/N) use the same model; any later `remove`/`purge`/`delete`/`rotate`/revoke-all is name-typed.

Verified under 24.22.2: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, fin-node `build` and `build:test`, `pnpm test` (926 passed, 5 skipped), `pnpm test:gates`, `pnpm gates`, `pnpm e2e`, `pnpm check:dep-age`, `tsc --noEmit` in finisherd, contracts and fin-node, `FIN_REQUIRE_SERVER=1 cargo test --workspace`; `node:sqlite` loads without a warning. **Other machines must switch**: install Node 24.22.2 (the Mac used nvm 26.12.1: `nvm install 24.22.2 && nvm use 24.22.2`), update `.superpowers/env.sh` (git-ignored, per machine), re-run `pnpm install`.

Also the codec patent posture (arch §26 question 1)

## Sub-project 5D: library indexer (planned 2026-11-07, revision 2 after review)

Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-07-sub-project-5d-library-indexer.md` (spec `docs/specs/2026-11-07-sub-project-5-control-plane-design.md` §7). One line per task; each task ticks its line with as-built notes.

- [x] Task 1: `fin-library` types, raw paths, names, placeholders, excludes, volume rules and the fake source. As built: the frozen wire types (`RawRel` as `{components: base64[]}`, `Expect`, `ScanEntry`, `DirDone`, `ScanBatch`, `SampleEntry`, `VolumeIdentity`, `RootState` tagged by `state`, `Placeholder`, `EntryType`, `OpenError`) generated into `@finisher/core-types`; nanosecond times as decimal strings, raw bytes and file ids as base64; WTF-8 for Windows names; NFC names and `relKey`s (folded with `to_lowercase` on case-insensitive roots); collision bits `nfc=1 | case=2 | lossy=4` (by the first key step at which two names meet); placeholder and reparse-tag rules over tagged `Attrs`; `listing::entries` applies every listing rule in one place (name-surrogate reparse points become `symlink`; a stub is reported as the name it stands for; a shadowed or second stub is `STUB_SHADOWED`); default excludes, an own glob matcher (unanchored patterns match names at any depth; `include` filters files only); `classify_root` and `volume::{key, strip_userinfo, sample}`; `Source`/`RawEntry`/`RootStat`/`DirHandle`; `FakeSource::from_json` with counters of placeholder touches and automount triggers. New dependency `unicode-normalization` 0.1.26; `base64` is 0.23 (the tree's), not 0.22. Conflict copies are matched by unambiguous markers only (Dropbox/Nextcloud `(conflicted copy …)`, Syncthing, ownCloud `_conflict-<digits>`, Google `[Conflict]`): `fin_store::conflict_names::classify` accepts only Finisher's own names, and iCloud's ` <n>`, OneDrive's `-<host>` and Google's `(<n>)` would drop real media
- [x] Task 2: Linux and macOS sources, volume identity and the opener. As built: `source::NativeSource` (directories by raw chain with `openat(O_NOFOLLOW | O_DIRECTORY)`, `O_NOATIME` on Linux until one `EPERM`; Linux names by `fdopendir`/`readdir` then `statx(AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW | AT_NO_AUTOMOUNT)` per entry, `STATX_ATTR_MOUNT_ROOT`/`AUTOMOUNT`, file ids = inode big-endian, `dev` = `major:minor`; macOS `getattrlistbulk` whose fixed-area end is found from the name's `attrreference_t`, `ATTR_DIR_MOUNTSTATUS` for mount points and triggers); `Source::open_child` added (a provided method: one open per directory instead of one per component); `RootStat` stats only the 16 sampled root entries on Linux; `volume::{identity, root_state, root_stat}` (`root_state` measures on its own thread, `Timeout` past the deadline), Linux key from the mountinfo line of `stx_mnt_id` (source for network and `fuse.*`, `f_fsid` locally), macOS `ATTR_VOL_UUID` / `f_mntfromname`; `open_for_read`: re-check without opening, `openat2(RESOLVE_BENEATH | NO_SYMLINKS | NO_MAGICLINKS | NO_XDEV)` with the per-component fallback on `ENOSYS`/`EPERM` (each component on the root's mount id), `O_NOATIME` only for files we own, `O_NONBLOCK` until verified (a FIFO never blocks), identity by size, mtime and inode; a missing file with a stub beside it is `CloudOnly`; **nested mounts below a root are not crossed** (`Escapes`, as Decision 19's `RESOLVE_NO_XDEV`); macOS `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY`, `getattrlistat(FSOPT_NOFOLLOW)` and `st_flags` checked again after the open; `process::{deny,allow}_dataless_materialisation`, `dataless_policy` (macOS `setiopolicy_np`/`getiopolicy_np`, declared locally); test hooks `open::hooks` (per thread: forced `openat2` errno and `O_NOATIME` `EPERM`, fallback counters); `examples/volume_identity.rs`. Step 0 (findings in `volume/mod.rs`): bind remount and ext4 loop remount keep every field; the same image on another loop device keeps the key (ext4 `f_fsid` from the UUID) while source and `st_dev` change; a new tmpfs changes `f_fsid`; a bind mount's unmount is visible only through `STATX_ATTR_MOUNT_ROOT`. Linux mount tests run in a user namespace through `unshare -Urm` or, where AppArmor restricts it (Ubuntu 24.04), `bwrap`. **Open:** the macOS run (only cross-compiled here: `disk_image_detached_is_offline`, `identity_stable_across_remount`, case-sensitive images, `dataless_dir_not_descended` with `FIN_TEST_ICLOUD=1`), NFS/CIFS remounts with `FIN_TEST_SHARE`, and a real idle automount (none on the Linux host; `automount_not_triggered` skips)
  - **CI note (5D merge into integration):** the Linux mount tests in `fin-library/tests/mounts_unix.rs` (`bind_mount_unmounted_is_offline`, `nested_mount_reported_and_crossed`, `identity_stable_across_remount`) need a user namespace that may mount. `support::userns` now asks `unshare -Urm`, then `bwrap`, to mount a tmpfs first and skips with a notice when neither can (Ubuntu's `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns = 1`, as on the Linux VM: `bwrap` starts, its children get no capabilities). **CI must set `FIN_REQUIRE_USERNS=1` on Linux runners where unprivileged user namespaces may mount**, so these tests fail rather than skip there.
- [x] Task 3: Windows source, volume identity and the opener. As built: `source::NativeSource` on Windows (`source/{windows, win, win_dir}.rs`): the root opened by its `\\?\` path (`CreateFileW`, backup semantics, followed as given), every directory below it by **raw name relative to its parent's handle** (`NtCreateFile` with `RootDirectory`, `FILE_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT | FILE_DIRECTORY_FILE`, share read/write/delete), so `CON`, `nul.txt`, trailing dots and spaces, unpaired surrogates (WTF-8) and paths past `MAX_PATH` need no Win32 path parsing; listings by `NtQueryDirectoryFileEx` on a fresh handle per query (`FileIdExtdDirectoryInformation` → `FileIdBothDirectoryInformation` → `FileFullDirectoryInformation`, stepped down per source at the first class refusal, the tag from `EaSize` in the fallbacks, 128-bit ids as stored, a 64-bit id zero-extended, an all-zero id `None`; `NativeSource::refuse_classes(n)` under test-hooks forces the chain); `stat_child` is a single-entry query for the **exact long name** (an 8.3 short name or another case never answers; names with wildcard characters are listed instead); `dir_mtime` from the handle (`FileBasicInfo`). Name-surrogate tags are listed `symlink` (never opened; `open_dir` of one fails `ERROR_STOPPED_ON_SYMLINK`); AF_UNIX, WSL FIFO/char/block and app-exec-link tags `other` (`reparse::is_special`); `dev` = the 64-bit volume serial (16 hex digits). **Measured and fixed:** NTFS lists a directory's times from its parent's index, which lags until that directory is next opened and closed, so the source reads each listed subdirectory's mtime from its own handle (one relative open per directory, never a placeholder one): without it the first scan after a change differed from the next and polls re-listed. **Placeholder rule change (Decision 13):** `placeholder::dir_from_attrs`/`attrs_of`: a directory is a placeholder only by `RECALL_ON_OPEN`, `RECALL_ON_DATA_ACCESS` or `OFFLINE`; `UNPINNED` alone on a directory ("free up space", measured: fully listed) is descended (used by `listing`, the poll path in `walk::run`, the fake source and both Windows modules). `volume::windows`: key = BLAKE3-16 of the serial, plus `\\server\share` (lower-cased, from `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`'s `\\?\UNC\` or `\Device\Mup\` form; a server part with userinfo is refused, WebDAV `@SSL`/`@port` kept) when `FileRemoteProtocolInfo` answers; `kind` from `GetVolumeInformationByHandleW`; `mount_root` = `VOLUME_NAME_NONE` is `\`; `case_sensitive` from `FileCaseSensitiveInfo` (NTFS reports `FILE_CASE_SENSITIVE_SEARCH` for every volume, so the volume flag is not used: a deviation from Decision 10's wording); `unreachable_code` maps the Cloud Files range to `CLOUD_ONLY` and network/device errors to `DISCONNECTED`. `open::windows`: components opened as themselves (a junction is `NameSurrogate`, a placeholder directory `CloudOnly`), the re-check is a directory query of the exact name (no open), the file opened from its parent **without** `FILE_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` (`FILE_READ_DATA`, share all, `FILE_SEQUENTIAL_ONLY`), then on the handle: tag and recall bits, `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW` (NT form) below the root's own final path and ending in the requested name, `Expect::check_volume` against the serial, size/`LastWriteTime`/id; residual window documented (Windows has no process policy that fails a read of a file evicted after the check). `process` stays `NotApplicable` on Windows. Removed: fin-workers' `LIBRARY_UNSUPPORTED` stub, `volume::root_stat`'s non-Unix error (fin-node's root probe and `libraryCanonicalRoot` now measure Windows roots), `cfg(unix)` on `tests/{probe, overlay}.rs` and fin-workers' `library_*` tests (Windows variants of the odd names and links), `walk_bench` runs on Windows. Tests: `tests/{source, open, cfapi}_windows.rs` (+ `win_support`); `cfapi_windows` registers a real sync root of its own (no admin) with `FETCH_DATA`/`FETCH_PLACEHOLDERS` counted and refused. **Step 0 findings** are at the top of `volume/windows.rs` (cloud-only file `0x9000401a` + `RECALL_ON_DATA_ACCESS`, hydrated `0x9000601a`, unpopulated directory `0x9000001a` + `OFFLINE | RECALL_ON_DATA_ACCESS`, unpinned directory `UNPINNED` only; zero callbacks across scans in all three classes and an opener pass; WOF hides its tag and reads through; `subst` removed → `NOT_FOUND`, re-added → same key, root id and sample). **Windows results** (Windows 11, NTFS): `cargo test -p fin-library` all targets green, `cargo test -p fin-workers` green (library family 4 + 13 + 11), clippy `-D warnings -D clippy::disallowed_methods` clean; walker determinism over the real source green. **100k warm scan** (`walk_bench`, release, 105,050 entries in 5,051 directories on NTFS): 0.26–0.32 s at 64 threads, 0.34–0.36 s at 8, 0.80–0.82 s at 1; unchanged poll 0.15 s, 0 re-listed. **Not measured here:** OneDrive (not running on the test host: our own sync root stands in), Data Deduplication (not installed), VHD attach (needs elevation), `FIN_TEST_SHARE` (UNC and mapped drives), a per-directory case-sensitive tree; left to Task 17. fin-node's napi and TS registry tests were not run on Windows
- [x] Task 4: the walker (parallel reads, timeouts, batches, polls, the test overlay). As built: `walk(src: Arc<dyn Source>, mode, &WalkOpts, sink, &CancelToken) -> Result<WalkSummary, WalkError>` (an `Arc`, not `&dyn`, so a read thread stuck past its deadline can be left behind); breadth-first by depth then raw chain, reads on up to `threads` lazily started threads, finished listings held in a window of `4 × threads` until every earlier directory is emitted, so batches are identical whatever the thread count. `WalkMode::Full {generation, known?}` (`gen` is reserved in edition 2024; `known` = the catalog's directories, the generated `KnownDir {dir, mtimeNs, entries, listedGen, mountRoot}` lines of `in/dirs.ndjson`, read by `KnownDirs::from_ndjson`; pass it on **every** full scan: it carries the mount and empty-suspect rules, and listings with `listedGen == generation` are skipped but descended), `Dirs {dirs, known?}` (exactly the given directories, minus excluded ones; with `known`, also subdirectories they report that the catalog does not know, e.g. a tree moved in), `Poll {known}` (known children re-stat'ed from the parent's handle with `Source::stat_child`; a known leaf costs one stat and no open; re-listed when the mtime changed, a known child vanished or is no longer a directory; new directories listed recursively; the root's own mtime by `Source::dir_mtime`). `Source` gained provided methods `read_dir_with(dir, progress)` (Linux: after the names and each `statx`; macOS: per `getattrlistbulk` buffer), `stat_child` (Linux `statx`, macOS `fstatat(AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)`; default lists the parent) and `dir_mtime` (default `None`: re-list); Task 3's Windows source should override all three. `Timing {firstStat 60 s, entry 30 s, flush 5 s}` (`flush`: a non-empty batch goes out at least that often). The first stat runs on its own thread (cancellable) and is judged by `classify_root` against `WalkOpts.stored`; offline, a known root with entries listing empty (`ROOT_EMPTY_SUSPECT`, in every mode, not only full scans), a failed or timed-out root listing, or as many stuck threads as `threads` end the walk with `WalkError::Offline(Box<RootState>)` (`.offline()`) before or between batches. A step-less read past `entry` gives `DirDone {complete: false, code: TIMEOUT}`; other not-descended directories get `CLOUD_ONLY` (placeholder), `AUTOMOUNT`, `ROOT_UNMOUNTED` (known mount root that is no longer one) or the open/listing error code; a listing that drops from N > 0 (known) to 0 is `DIR_EMPTY_SUSPECT` and incomplete. Batches ≤ `batch_entries` (4,096) entries + `DirDone`s and ≤ `batch_bytes` (8 MiB, estimated from above); a listing's `DirDone` lands with its last entry; `seq` from 0 per walk; a cancel stops within one batch; a sink error is `WalkError::Stopped`. Kept directory handles are capped at 64 (finished listings waiting included; leaf listings keep none), so a wide tree stays under macOS's 256-descriptor default (`descriptors_stay_bounded_on_wide_trees`). `overlay` (test-hooks): `.fin-fake.json` at a real root (placeholders by attribute bit, automounts, mount roots, unmounts, read and per-entry delays, open/list errors, first-stat delay and error) via `overlay::wrap`, and `open_for_read` logs every open of a listed path to `openLog` (absolute, outside the root) and refuses marked placeholders unless `hydrate`. `CancelToken` is this crate's (`from_flag(Arc<AtomicBool>)` for Task 6's bridge). `serde_json` is now a normal dependency. **Measured** (walker alone, sink not serialising; `examples/walk_bench.rs`, release, this Linux host: 10 cores, ext4 on LVM): 100,000 files in 5,050 directories **warm** 0.07 s at 64 threads (0.07 s at 8, 0.30–0.46 s at 1), unchanged poll of 5,051 directories 0.011–0.021 s; **cold not measured** (dropping caches needs root here; the controller records it in Task 17). Notes for later tasks: Task 6 lists `overlay.rs` (reads its own file with `std::fs`) in `clippy-allow.json` and builds the source as `overlay::wrap(root, NativeSource)` under test-hooks; Task 7b also applies N → 0 from the catalog (a full scan without `known` reports `complete: true, entries: 0`) and treats `DIR_EMPTY_SUSPECT` as its candidate listing; a poll sees no in-place content change (directory mtimes do not move), which the daily or weekly full scan catches
- [x] Task 5: `fin-probe` entry points, `PROBE_VERSION`, library probe and hash. As built: `fin_probe::probe_reader(r: impl Read + Seek, FileMeta {size, mtimeNs}, path) -> ProbeOutcome {fingerprint: Option<String>, probe: Result<Probe, CoreError>}` (the `fp1` first, so a failed probe keeps it; every kind reader is generic over `Read + Seek`, the MP4 path included), `probe_with(path, open: impl FnOnce(&Path) -> io::Result<File>)` (one open, metadata, fingerprint and probe from that handle) and `probe(path) = probe_with(path, File::open)`, `fingerprint_reader`, **`PROBE_VERSION = 1`** (stored per file; **sub-project 6 (6A-2 on) and 8 bump it whenever probe output changes for the same bytes**). `fin-library`: `Throttle {budget: Option<fin_jobs::ReadBudget>, cancel: Option<CancelToken>}` and `BudgetedReader` (every byte read is taken from the bucket; a set cancel fails the next read), `FileRef {file, root, rel, expect, hydrate, display}`, `probe_file(&FileRef, &Throttle) -> ProbeOutcome {file, fingerprint, probe, probe_version, state, code}` (`open_for_read` once, `probe_reader` over the throttled handle) and `hash_file -> HashOutcome {file, hash: "blake3:<hex>", state, code}` (whole file, 256 KiB reads; a size at EOF other than `expect.size` is `changed`). Outcome states: the catalog's `present | missing | cloudOnly | unreadable | probeFailed` plus two that are **never stored**: `changed` (code `FILE_CHANGED`; nothing read, the catalog rescans the directory) and `cancelled` (the worker drops it); codes `FILE_CLOUD_ONLY`, `FILE_NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_PATH`, `ASSET_UNREADABLE`, else the probe's own. **Rule for Task 13 (frozen with Decision 20):** `asset.probe`/`asset.add` of a library file must call `fin_probe::probe_with(path, |_| open_for_read(root, rel, expect, hydrate)...)` with the catalogued row (the closure maps `OpenError` to `io::Error`; keep the `OpenError` out of band to answer `FILE_CLOUD_ONLY`/`FILE_CHANGED`), never `probe(path)` or `fingerprint(path)`. The extension filter of Decision 37 (`skipped`) is the worker's, not here. `fin-library` now depends on `fin-probe` and `fin-jobs` (workspace crates, no new external dependency). Tests: `fin-probe/tests/reader.rs` (5), `fin-library/tests/probe.rs` (9)
- [x] Task 6: the `library` worker family and the clippy gate. As built: `fin-workers library` (`src/library/{mod, scan, probe, hash, common, jobfiles}.rs`) serving `library.scan|probe|hash` v1; kinds added to `JOB_KINDS`. **Params and results live in `fin-library::params`** (not `fin-workers`, a binary-only package `export_schema` cannot link): `LibraryScanParams {library, root, mode: full|dirs|poll, gen, dirs?, excludes, threads?, volume?, timing? (test-hooks builds only; a release worker refuses it)}`, `LibraryProbeParams {library, root, hydrate, readBps?, volume, extensions?, files, items}`, `LibraryHashParams` (the same without `extensions`), results `LibraryScanResult`/`LibraryProbeResult`/`LibraryHashResult` (counts by state, `datalessPolicy`), `FileLine {id, rel, expect}` (one line of `in/files.ndjson`), `ProbedCall`, `BatchCall`, `PARAMS` (the `KindParams` table for `validateJobParams`) and `register_schemas` (in `export_schema`; types regenerated). **Beyond the plan's lists:** `volume` (the library's stored identity: the scan's first stat is judged against it, and probe and hash re-judge the root before the job and whenever a file comes back missing or unreadable, `RootWatch`), `items` (decimal file ids; `restrict` narrows `items`, not `in/files.ndjson`), `extensions` (the Decision 37 filter: absent = `kinds: all`; a file whose extension is not listed is `skipped` with code `KIND_FILTER`, never opened), and `hydrate` on hash. **Batch file (Task 7a's format, `catalog/batch-file.ts`):** `out/scan-<seq:08>.ndjson`, NDJSON in which **each line is one generated `ScanBatch`** (`{seq, entries, dirsDone}`); the worker writes one line per file (one walker batch), ≤ 4,096 entries and ≤ 8 MiB (`fin_library::batchfile::{encode, decode, file_name, MAX_ENTRIES, MAX_BYTES}`; `encode` refuses a batch past either cap), closes it, `call library.batch {file, seq}`, and removes it after the reply (the reply means the indexer applied it). **`in/dirs.ndjson`** is read in 7a's form, one line per present or unreadable directory `{dir: RawRel, mtimeNs: string | null, listed: bool}` (`KnownDirs::from_ndjson(reader, generation)`: `listed` becomes `listed_gen = generation`, so a resumed full scan skips and still descends it); the richer `KnownDir` keys (`entries`, `listedGen`, `mountRoot`, `volume`) are accepted and optional. **Gap for Tasks 7a/9/10:** without `entries`, `mountRoot` and `volume` in those lines the walker's own N > 0 → 0 (`DIR_EMPTY_SUSPECT`, `ROOT_EMPTY_SUSPECT`) and nested-mount rules (`ROOT_UNMOUNTED`, `ROOT_VOLUME_CHANGED`) stay inert in a worker; 7b's catalog side covers the empty rules, and `writeKnownDirs` should add `entries`, `mountRoot` and `volume` (stored from `DirDone.volume`) before Task 9 relies on the nested-mount checks. **Also for 7a's validator:** the generated `DirDone` carries an optional `volume` (a nested mount's identity) that `DirDoneSpec` in `batch-file.ts` does not list yet; its drift test fails once this branch is merged until the spec adds `volume: opt(nullOr(object(VolumeIdentitySpec)))`. **Ordering (Task 7b):** a listing's entries all precede the `DirDone` that closes it, in the same line or an earlier one or file, and `DirDone.entries` counts every entry emitted for that listing, excluded ones included (`dir_done_counts_every_entry_and_follows_them`). `call library.probed {results ≤ 32}` is followed by one `item(id)` (no value) per covered file; a hash item's value is `blake3:<hex>` (failures are item errors with the outcome's code); nothing is acknowledged when the call fails. **Task 5's two never-stored states:** `changed` (`FILE_CHANGED`) travels in `library.probed` unchanged and is counted in the result (`changed`): **Task 7a's `applyProbed` must not store it and Task 10 rescans that file's directory (`mode: dirs`)**; `cancelled` is dropped, its item unacknowledged, the job ends `JOB_CANCELLED` after flushing the finished files. A missing or unreadable file in a root that no longer judges online (`RootWatch`, trusted for 2 s after a pass) is dropped, the finished files are flushed and the job answers `LIBRARY_OFFLINE {state}` with `retry: never`; scans answer it for a root the walker finds offline (`WalkError::Offline`). Progress: one message per completed directory, thinned to ≤ 64 per batch; a pause checkpoints `{paused: true}` (the catalog, with `gen` and `in/dirs.ndjson`, is the scan's resume state). **Dataless policy:** `fin-jobs` gained `Family::init` (after the self-sandbox entry, before `hello`; an `Err` exits `EXIT_FAMILY_INIT` = 71) and the family denies materialisation there; only a cold `hydrate: true` probe or hash job allows it again; results report the OS's policy (`off` on macOS). **Note for Task 9 (isolation):** a probe group of up to 32 finished files is acknowledged only after its `library.probed` reply, so a crash leaves up to 32 `begin`s without `item`: the engine's "in flight" must treat that as a set (bisect it, then the poison file alone); one file per `library.probed` call would make it exactly one at the cost of a host round trip per file. **Gate:** `clippy.toml` (`disallowed-methods`: the 19 std methods of Decision 21 and `dunce::canonicalize`) in `fin-library`, `fin-workers`, `fin-node` and `fin-media` (`fin-analyze` when it exists); `scripts/check-clippy-config.mjs` (+ test) fails a listed crate without the config (`CLIPPY_CONFIG`), an `allow`/`expect` of the lint in a file `scripts/clippy-allow.json` does not list (`CLIPPY_ALLOW_UNLISTED`, an entry is a file or a directory with a reason) and an entry that allows nothing (`CLIPPY_ALLOW_STALE`); `pnpm gates` runs it and `cargo clippy -p <the listed crates> --all-targets -- -D clippy::disallowed_methods`. Allow-list: `overlay.rs`, `volume/linux.rs` (`/proc/self/mountinfo`), `jobfiles.rs` (the job's own `in/*.ndjson`), fin-node's `library/mod.rs` (`real_directory`) plus its fonts, journal, private-dir and server-lock sites (server data and project files, never a library path), the test families, and the test directories. Test overlay: `crashOnOpen` aborts the process at an `open_for_read` of the path. Windows: `NativeSource` is Unix-only until Task 3, so a scan there answers `LIBRARY_UNSUPPORTED` (removed by Task 3). `family_starts_no_process` is a source check (no `Command` or `process::exit` in `fin-library` and the family) plus, on Linux, a `/proc/<pid>/task/*/children` poll during a running scan. Not run here: `family_denies_dataless_before_hello` on macOS (it asserts `datalessPolicy == off` there). Tests: `fin-workers/tests/library_{scan,probe,family}.rs` (11, 13, 5), `fin-library/tests/batchfile.rs` (3), `scripts/check-clippy-config.test.mjs` (11)
- [x] Task 7a: catalogs: extensions, schema, indexer and reader threads. As built (`apps/finisherd/src/library/catalog/`; 5A's `home/sp5a-principals` merged in first for `control/migrations.ts`): `registerCatalogExtension({name, module: URL})` (a `file:` URL, before the threads start; `core` is registered first and loaded the same way) and `CatalogExtensionModule {migrations, versions?, ops, queries}`; both threads `import()` every module, and each extension is a chain run by 5A's `planMigrations`/`applyMigrations` (so the catalog's table is `migrations(chain, n)`, not `(ext, n)`). `startCatalogThreads` / `layerCatalogs` start `library-indexer` and `library-reader`; `IndexerClient` (`exec`, `open` = indexer open then reader attach, `close`, `applyBatchFile(lib, path, gen, seq?)`, `finishScan`, `applyProbed`, `applyHashes`, `stagesSet`, `writeKnownDirs(lib, gen, path)`, `pending`, `purgeMissing(lib, before)`, `stats`) and `ReaderClient` (`query`, `files`, `file`, `search`, `lookup`, `counts`, `stageCounts`, `stats`), all `Effect<_, FinError>`. Arguments are checked by `assertCloneable` before posting (functions, symbols, class instances, `Buffer`s and accessors refused: `CATALOG_ARG_NOT_CLONEABLE`); internal codes `CATALOG_NOT_OPEN`, `CATALOG_TOO_NEW`, `CATALOG_ARGS_INVALID`, `CATALOG_EXTENSION_UNKNOWN`, `CATALOG_OP_UNKNOWN`, `CATALOG_OP_FAILED`, `CATALOG_RESULT_NOT_CLONEABLE`, `CATALOG_THREAD_EXITED`, `LIBRARY_BATCH_INVALID {reason}` (Task 13 adds the user-facing ones to the lists). The indexer serialises each library's requests and interleaves libraries, ≤ 2,000 rows per transaction with an event-loop turn between them; the reader uses `readOnly` connections plus `query_only`. Schema as Decision 23 plus a root `dirs` row (id 1) and **`files.probe BLOB`**: a format byte then deflate-raw of the probe JSON without the fields the row holds (`core/probe-codec.ts`). **Batch file format for Task 6:** NDJSON in the job's `out/`, each line one generated `ScanBatch`, ≤ 4,096 entries and ≤ 8 MiB per file, opened `O_NOFOLLOW` with an `lstat`/`fstat` identity check, validated field for field against the generated schema (a drift test compares them); an entry whose directory is neither catalogued nor created earlier in the file fails the file (`unknownDir`), before any write. **`in/dirs.ndjson` for Tasks 4/6:** one line per present or unreadable directory, `{dir: RawRel, mtimeNs: string | null, listed: bool}`. 7a's apply (`core/apply.ts`) upserts rows by raw name, clears `fp`, `probe`, `probeVersion`, `fullHash`, `kind` and the media columns on a change, records a directory's `mtimeNs` only from its own complete listing, and skips excluded files, symlinks and others; missing, renames, moves, hard links and collisions are 7b's. **Measured** (`catalog-size.test.ts`, 100k files in 5k directories, names in five scripts with NFD twins, every file probed with a typical MP4 probe): **970 B/file** with the FTS `dir` column; FTS 126 B/file of which the `dir` column 75; the probe JSON was ~540 B and took the catalog to 1,205 B/file before it was stored deflated (286 B). So directory tokens stay in `dir`, no `dirnames` table; margin ~50 B, which Task 15 re-checks with real probes (`dirnames` would buy back ~75 B). Scan apply through batch files ran at ~14.7k entries/s on this VM (FTS inserts are about half the cost), under Task 15's 25k/s budget: Task 15 (or an FTS tuning follow-up) must close that
- [x] Task 7b: the diff and its property test. As built (`apps/finisherd/src/library/catalog/core/{diff.ts, collisions.ts, apply.ts}`, `core` step **0002**: `files.dev`, `dirs.emptyJob`, partial index `files_rename`; `formatVersion.core` 2, `minReaderVersion` 1): **listings** are per `(job, dir)`, the job being the directory that holds the batch file's `out/`; each remembers the transaction time of its first entry (`start`) and how many entries it saw, and its `DirDone {complete: true}` marks missing the rows with `lastSeen < start` (not seen by it, nor by any other listing since it began, so a poll and a full scan of one directory never undo each other) and absent subdirectories with their subtrees; transaction times never go backwards per catalog (`max(at, lastAt)`, `lastAt` from the catalog at open). Nothing is marked when the listing is incomplete, the library is offline (`IndexerClient.setOffline`, persisted as `meta.offline`), it saw fewer entries than `DirDone.entries` (state lost to a restart or a rollback), or it is empty while the directory holds live rows: `DIR_EMPTY_SUSPECT` (`emptySince`, `emptyJob`), confirmed only by **another job's** empty listing ≥ 60 s later; a suspect listing records no `mtimeNs`/`listedGen`, so the next poll and a resumed scan list it again. An empty complete **root** listing while any live file exists fails the whole batch file before any write (`ROOT_EMPTY_SUSPECT`; the service puts the library offline, Task 10); the root never takes the 60 s confirmation. **Renames** (`dev:fileId` with equal size and mtime; an all-zero id counts as none and is stored `NULL`): a new name matching a missing row takes that row over (latest `missingSince`, then lowest id); matching a live row it is inserted with `renameOf` (lowest id); when the old row's directory closes a listing without it the old row takes the new path, keeping its id, `firstSeen`, `lastChange` and its probe if it had a fingerprint (else the new row's), and the new row is deleted; a listing that saw the old row clears `renameOf`. `hardlink` is derived per transaction: a live row sharing `dev:fileId` with another live row. **Moves** (`applyProbed`, now answering `moved: [{from, to}]`): a row's first fingerprint, on a row unchanged since insert and with no pending rename, equal to a missing row's makes the missing row take the new path and this probe. A missing row back with its size and mtime is present with its fingerprint (re-probed if it had none), whatever its file id; for live rows ids count as a change only when both are known. A placeholder directory's subtree becomes `cloudOnly`; an excluded directory's subtree goes missing (not while offline). **Collisions** are recomputed per closed listing over files and subdirectories together (bits as `fin_library::collide`: nfc 1, case 2, lossy 4, decoding with WHATWG UTF-8, which matches `from_utf8_lossy`); `counts` answers `collisions` (live flagged rows, for `NAME_COLLISION {count}`), `ReaderClient.resolve(lib, relKey)` answers the one row (live rows first), `PATH_AMBIGUOUS {fileIds}` or `FILE_NOT_FOUND`. `purgeMissing` also deletes missing directories left empty (`{deleted, dirs}`). **For Task 4:** `DirDone.entries` must count every entry the job emitted for that listing (all types, excluded ones included); a listing's entries all precede its `DirDone` in the same job's files. **For Task 13:** `PATH_AMBIGUOUS` (3), `ROOT_EMPTY_SUSPECT`, `DIR_EMPTY_SUSPECT`, `NAME_COLLISION` go into the code lists. **Fixed on the way (7a latent):** a deletion from the contentless-delete FTS table left a tombstone rowid above 2^53 as the connection's last insert rowid, so the next plain `.run()` on the connection threw (`purgeMissing` of > 500 rows failed); FTS statements answer bigints and `ftsSettle` resets the rowid after deletions, commits and rollbacks. **Tests:** `diff.test.ts` (17 cases, the plan's twelve plus the offline flag across a restart, poll-vs-scan, subtree, placeholder and zero-id cases) and `diff.property.test.ts` (`diff_matches_reference_model`, fast-check 4.10.2 (MIT, dev, the pin 5C-1 uses): random worlds with colliding names, ids/no ids/zero ids, case-sensitive or not, renames, hard links, copy-moves, rewrites, deletions and restores, placeholder flips, unreadable and emptied directories, full scans, polls, a poll interleaved with a full scan, interrupted scans resumed through `writeKnownDirs` (its file is checked too), probes, clock jumps and offline flips, through the real indexer thread and through `test/library/support/model.ts`, a brute-force model sharing no code; after every step both agree row for row, ids paired, FTS rowids too; 150 runs by default (~7 s; `FIN_DIFF_RUNS` ≤ 2,000), every rule kind must occur). Mutants of the confirmation delay, the clearing of `renameOf` by a listing that saw the row, the `lastSeen < start` mark and the missing-candidate tie-break each fail it. Scratch directories live in `<tmp>/finisher-catalog-tests/<pid>-*`, each run of the property test deletes its catalog and jobs, and a new scratch removes those of dead processes (a killed run had left 6 GB). **Measured** (100k tree, `catalog-size.test.ts`, through the threads, alone on this VM): scan apply **38.6k entries/s** (7a: 14.7k; Task 15's budget 25k), 969 B/file. The cost was not FTS: `INSERT … RETURNING` cost ~55 µs a row (now `run()` + `lastInsertRowid`), per-entry rename lookups (now one `json_each` query per transaction), statement journals in temp files (`temp_store = MEMORY`) and a collision reset per listing (now only when a row is flagged); in-process a first scan runs ~50k/s and a rescan ~55k/s.
  - **Review fixes (7a/7b, `sp5d-t7-fix`).** (1) **Listing start is the read time, not the apply time:** `IndexerClient.attemptStart(lib)` answers a catalog time strictly after every write so far (kept in `meta.clock`, so it survives a close) and `applyBatchFile(lib, path, {gen, startedAt, seq?})` takes it; a listing's `start` is `min(first entry's transaction time, startedAt)`, so a full scan's batch applied after a poll no longer marks the poll's new file missing; `dirs.listedAt` records the start of the listing whose `mtimeNs`/`listedGen` are kept, and an older listing applied later records neither; the empty confirmation needs the confirming listing's `start` ≥ `emptySince` (the suspect's apply time) + 60 s. The batch format is unchanged (no `listedAtMs` in `DirDone`, no Rust or generated-type change). **For Task 6:** the scan kind takes `attemptStart` in `prepare` at each attempt, before the worker reads anything, and passes it with every `applyBatchFile` of that attempt (a wall-clock attempt start also works, but then a clock jump makes the diff mark less until the clock catches up). (2) The property test now has a ground-truth check that does not rest on the model: every row that went missing in a step (except `EXCLUDED`) has a complete listing applied in that step, of its directory or an ancestor, for which the path toward the row was absent from the world from the listing's read to its apply. New steps: `stale` (a full scan reads, the world changes, a poll is applied, then the scan's batch), `crash`/`resume {restart}` (a real catalog close and reopen mid-scan, so listings are lost, then a new job name), `fault` (`IndexerClient.testFault`, test hooks only: a transaction rolled back mid-file), `exclude` (exclusion by glob, not name, so a known directory becomes excluded), backward clock jumps; 3 rows per transaction; per-kind minimums scaled to the run count. Mutants of the listing start (in the catalog alone and in catalog and model together: the ground truth catches it), the suspicion clearing and the `listedAt` freshness gate each fail it. 150 runs ~15 s; soak from the root `FIN_DIFF_RUNS=2000 pnpm exec vitest run apps/finisherd/test/library/diff.property.test.ts` (~2 min; `FIN_DIFF_NO_SHRINK=1` reports a failure's seed at once, `FIN_DIFF_SEED`/`FIN_DIFF_PATH` replay it). The first 1,000-run soak found a 7b bug that predates these fixes: when one listing closed with two vanished sibling directories and the second held a pending rename of a row in the first, the catalog resolved the rename while marking the first subtree (the new row still live) and merged the two rows, which depended on the siblings' order; renames now resolve only once all of a listing's marks are done (the model's rule), pinned as the property test's `REGRESSION` example and `diff.test.ts` "a pending rename between two vanished siblings…". (3) Untrusted names and keys (`core/entry-check.ts`, before any write): every raw component per `RawRel::validate` (non-empty, not `.`/`..`, no `/` or NUL, nor `\` on Windows), `nameLossy` = not valid UTF-8, `nameNfc` canonically equivalent to the `from_utf8_lossy` decoding (both normalised in JS, so a Unicode-version gap cannot refuse a good name), `relKey` = the parent's key + `/` + `nameNfc` or a case fold of it; else `LIBRARY_BATCH_INVALID {reason: rawName | nameLossy | nameNfc | relKey, row}`. (4) Any entry applied under a directory clears its `DIR_EMPTY_SUSPECT` (`emptySince`, `emptyJob`, the code). (5) A known directory or file reported excluded goes missing with code **`EXCLUDED`** (a directory with its subtree) only once that listing closes completely; `EXCLUDED` rows are never a move's or a `dev:fileId` takeover's old row, and their pending renames are not resolved (plan Decisions 16, 25). (6) Subtree changes (`markSubtree`, `cloudSubtree`, the hard-link settle) run as generators in steps of ≤ `txRows` rows with event-loop turns between them inside one transaction (`core/steps.ts`), so readers see them whole; `stats().maxStepRows` reports the largest step (test: 6,000 rows under an excluded directory, 100-row steps, one transaction, readers saw only before or after). Low items: only batch-apply failures drop the open listings (`exec` failures no longer do); index `files_order (state, lastChange, id)` for `pending`; the default clock is `guardedClock()` (`clock.ts`: forward jumps held to the monotonic pace for 10 minutes, then accepted); `purgeMissing(lib, {before, beforeGen})` also needs `files.missingGen < beforeGen` (the service passes `currentGen - 1`, so a full scan must have run since); `files.missingGen`, `dirs.listedAt` and `files_order` went into `core` step 0002 (not merged anywhere yet); batch files are opened under `out/`'s real path, which must lie under the jobs root's real path, and `out/` must keep its device and inode until the file is open; `jobsRoot` is required (`layerCatalogs` takes no test hooks); a catalog thread that dies after start calls `onFatal` once (default: a stderr line) and `Catalogs.health()` answers `{ok: false, error}`, every call failing with its `CATALOG_THREAD_EXITED`/`CATALOG_THREAD_FAILED` (not restarted); `reader_never_waits_for_writer` asks a median < 20 ms, ≤ 2 of 10 reads ≥ 20 ms and the worst < 250 ms (all < 20 ms with `FIN_STRICT_TIMING=1`). New codes for Task 13: `EXCLUDED` (a missing code), `CATALOG_TEST_FAULT` (tests only).
  - **Contract with the walker and the worker (Tasks 4, 6; `home/sp5d-t4-macos` merged in for the generated `DirDone.volume`, `VolumeIdentity`, `SampleEntry`, `KnownDir`).** The batch validator accepts `DirDone.volume` (`VolumeIdentity` field for field, `sample` ≤ 64 entries; `SPECS` gains `VolumeIdentity` and `SampleEntry`, so the drift test covers them); a fresh complete listing stores it in `dirs.volume` (JSON) and its count in `dirs.entries` (`core` 0002). A `DirDone {complete: false}` with code `ROOT_UNMOUNTED` or `ROOT_VOLUME_CHANGED` makes the directory and its subtree (directories and files) `offline` in steps, with that code; no listing below an offline directory marks anything (it counts `incomplete`), an offline directory absent from its parent's listing is not marked missing, the parent's listing keeps it offline, and `dirs.mount` sticks (a parent listing that reports the mount point as a plain directory does not clear it); only the mount point's own complete listing brings it back to `present` (its files come back as they are seen, the rest goes missing as usual). `in/dirs.ndjson` lines are now `{dir, mtimeNs, listed, entries?, mountRoot, volume?}` (the keys `KnownDirs::from_ndjson` reads; `entries` absent for a directory never listed completely) and include offline directories, so the walker's N → 0 and nested-mount checks have their inputs. Not done: clearing `mount` for a mount the owner removed for good (it stays a mount root reporting `ROOT_UNMOUNTED`; the service needs an accept step like `acceptVolume`, Task 10), and the `ROOT_UNMOUNTED {dir}`/`ROOT_VOLUME_CHANGED {dir}` warnings in `library.status` (Task 13).
  - Deferred, not fixed here: unstable SMB file ids (Decision 22's fallback detection: a server whose ids change across listings) are not detected; ids are trusted as hints, which at worst turns a rename into a missing row plus a new one.
  - Seen while running the checks, not 7b's: `crates/fin-core/tests/rebase_moves_props.rs` (`stale_and_fresh_agree_with_moves_links_transitions`) found a new failing case on this base, stale refused with `INSERT_INSIDE_CLIP`: `cc 9cac2329ae0d6661246ed5fbc4271c0a8f20466a2bb18ed2f05330712a581c83 # shrinks to lens = [[10, 10, 10], [], [], [10]], linked = false, trs = false, committed = [([Move { track: 0, clip: 4, to: 0, before: None, overwrite: false }], false)], mine = [Slide { track: 0, clip: 2, frames: -1 }, Move { track: 3, clip: 0, to: 0, before: Some(4), overwrite: false }]` (not committed to the regressions file here, so the suite stays green; for the fin-core owner)

A stage job of another family that ends `LIBRARY_OFFLINE` puts the library offline like the library kinds' (Decision 33). **Versions:** at attach (and at registration) `stagesStale` marks other versions' rows `stale`; only that stage re-runs. **Restart:** `queued`/`running` rows are reconciled with `engine.get(jobId)` per job (active → adopted, waiters included; finished → settled; gone → `stale` `JOB_LOST`), active probe jobs adopted; rows of a stage not registered yet wait for its registration. **`prioritize(fp, stage)`:** unknown stage `INVALID_REQUEST`, no online library holding the fp (and running the stage) `FILE_NOT_FOUND`; in flight or done → nothing; dependencies not done are prioritized first and the stage is held out of background batches until they are done, then submitted alone at `normal` (past `maxInFlight` and the retry hour). **`stageStates(fp)`:** per library holding a live file, `probe` (its `probeVersion`) then its rows. **`locate`** (`locate.ts`): an absolute path under a root or alias (longest base wins; Windows-style bases compare with `\` as `/` and without case; `.`/`..` → `INVALID_PATH`), key folded as `fin_library::names::fold`, several live rows → `PATH_AMBIGUOUS {fileIds}`; then fingerprint, then full hash (present rows first, then by library order); As built (`apps/finisherd/src/library/{search.ts, lookup.ts}`, `catalog/core/{search.ts, snapshot.ts}`, tests `test/library/search.test.ts`, 15 cases). **Reader thread:** `ReaderClient.searchPage({libraries, args, cursor?, limit ≤ 200})` (a new reader-thread method, not an extension query: it spans connections) → `{hits, next?, truncated}`; `Snapshots` (`core/snapshot.ts`) keeps the first page's ranking of **≤ 2,000 hits** (each library asked for 2,001; merged by `(score, library id, row id)`; `truncated` when more matched) under `s1.<token>.<offset>`, **5 minutes after the last use** (sliding), **≤ 64** live (least recently used out), none when the answer fits one page; a query **without terms** pages by a stateless keyset `k1.<base64url {sig, lastChange, library, id}>` over `(lastChange desc, library id, row id)`, `score` 0, nothing kept. A cursor carries a signature of the query, filters and sorted library list: another query is `CURSOR_INVALID`, a dropped snapshot `CURSOR_EXPIRED`. Hits are lean rows (`library, fileId, relKey, display, kind?, size, durationMs?, width?, height?, fp?, state, collision, score, offline, lastChange`), `display` the NFC path in the names' true case; `offline` = the catalog's persisted `meta.offline` (`IndexerClient.setOffline`) or file state `offline`. **Query semantics:** NFC first; terms are runs of `\p{L}\p{N}\p{M}\p{Co}` (as `unicode61` splits; a mark-only run is dropped), each a quoted prefix term (`"interv"*`) over `name` and `dir`, all required, so no FTS syntax survives (`AND`, `NEAR`, `col:`, `-`, `^`, `*` are words or separators); ≤ 16 terms and ≤ 512 characters (`SEARCH_QUERY_INVALID`); ranked `bm25(names, 4.0, 1.0)` (a name hit beats a folder hit). Case and diacritics fold (`cafe`/`CAFÉ`/NFD finds `Café`; Cyrillic, Greek, Latin diacritics); Han/kana/Hangul/Thai runs are one token each and match by their prefix only (no word segmentation; `ビュー` does not find `インタビュー`); `ß` does not fold to `ss`. Filters: `kind`, `state`, `since` (`lastChange ≥`), ranges on `durationMs`, `width`, `height` and (beyond the plan) `size`; without `state`, `missing` rows are skipped. **Lookup:** `lookupLibraries(reader, {libraries, fingerprints | hashes ≤ 1000, hashCandidates?}, hash?)` answers `{matches: [{key, library, fileId, relKey, display, state}], pending?}` in request-key order (rows of any state, with `state`); `hashCandidates` hashes **present rows without a full hash whose size equals a match's** (`core.hashCandidates`, ≤ 1,000 per library, `more` says the cap cut) through the injected `HashSubmit` (`Libraries.hash`): **one job per library**, `pending: {job (the first), jobs: [{library, job}], more}` (the plan's singular `pending.job` is the first; Task 13 decides the wire shape). `core.lookup` gained `display`.   - **Review fixes (Tasks 9–11, `sp5d-t11-fix` on `sp5d-t11`).** (1) **Linux watches** are fin-node's `libraryWatchStart` (`fin_library::watch`): one inotify watch per directory on a thread of its own, on the root's mount only (`statx` with `AT_NO_AUTOMOUNT`, `same_mount`: no nested mount descended, no automount triggered), never a dot-directory, opened `O_NOFOLLOW` and watched by descriptor; every failure (`WATCH_LIMIT`, `ENOSPC`, `EACCES`, `WATCH_ROOT_GONE`, a queue overflow re-walks and polls) is reported and degrades the library, never a partial watch. Node 24.22.2's recursive watcher (checked in `internal/fs/recursive_watch`) watches directories only on Linux and throws a walk failure out of `fs.watch`, but a failure while adding a directory created later throws from a child watcher's callback (uncaught in the indexer thread), and it descends mounts and dot-directories and walks on the indexer thread; it stays only on macOS and Windows (native FSEvents/`ReadDirectoryChangesW`, `ignore` for dot paths). Before a Linux watch starts, the catalog's live directories (+25% + 256) must fit `fs.inotify.max_user_watches` less 10% and what the thread's other watches reserved (`WATCH_LIMIT`). Watched libraries get a 6-hour safety-net poll. (2) `goOffline` stops the watch (`watch: none`), `goOnline` starts it again. (3) `acceptVolume` probes first and refuses an unmounted or empty root (`LIBRARY_ROOT_SUSPECT`) unless `force`; until a full scan succeeds a catalog guard (`setDropGuard`, `meta.dropGuard`) refuses a root listing that would mark more than half of the root's live directories missing (`ROOT_DROP_SUSPECT`: the library goes offline, re-probes never lift it, `acceptVolume {force: true}` confirms); `CATALOG_*` reasons survive `goOnline` and later offline causes. (4) `DirsQueue` (`dirs-queue.ts`): one queued `dirs` job per library; a request already covered answers it, one with new directories cancels it and submits the union at the higher priority (the merged-away job is not journaled); past 1,000 directories a poll. Freshness: a directory listed by a targeted scan within 5 minutes is fresh, and a listing asks for its directory at most once a minute (else `stale: true`). (5) Stage items failing `FILE_CHANGED`/`FILE_NOT_FOUND`/`FILE_CLOUD_ONLY` cost no attempt and their directories are rescanned. (6) `library.probed` refuses any `probeVersion` but `libraryProbeVersion()`. (7) A scan or stage job that ends before its submit answers settles (the service and the pump ask `engine.get` once it is recorded; a stage job's end is held until its `queued` rows are written). (8) Nothing is submitted for an offline library; a probe or hash failing `LIBRARY_OFFLINE` while offline keeps the re-probe cadence. (9) No-change targeted and watch scans are counted (`noChangePolls`), not journaled. (10) A prioritized stage whose dependency failed for good is dropped from `#wanted`. (11) `locate(ref, reach?)` filters the libraries searched. (12) `recordScan` refuses a `library.scan` payload with unknown fields (the journal's typed decode drops them anyway; tested both). Follow-up: the mount-exclusion test (`watch_linux.rs` `nested_mounts_are_not_watched`) needs a user namespace that may mount; here it was run as root in `unshare -m`.

Both compositors now share `placement` (quad, inverse, level, bounds, transition weight) and the layer walk with its skips (`source::walk`, `readable`); T0 hashes unchanged. **Mip levels are separate textures** sized like the reference chain (⌈w/2⌉; WebGPU levels round down), and the layer shader mixes `textureSampleLevel(…, 0)` of ⌊L⌋ and ⌈L⌉ by the fraction.   - [x] Task 10: `fin-shm`. As built: `JobDir {root}` (`resolve(root)` canonicalises only the host's `jobs/` parent; `input()`, `output()`, `helper_output(id)`), `checked_open(path, cap) -> File` (absolute paths only; every segment opened no-follow from the previous one's descriptor: unix `openat(O_NOFOLLOW|O_DIRECTORY)`, Linux `O_PATH`; Windows `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` per segment, held without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`, reparse points refused, final path re-checked with `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`; the file `fstatat`-checked before it is opened, so a FIFO or device is never opened, then opened `O_NONBLOCK` and re-checked on the descriptor; regular, `nlink == 1`, ≤ cap), `commit_output(from, to, cap)` (rename from the checked parent descriptor, then the arrival is verified as the same inode with one link and under the cap, else removed; across volumes a bounded `O_EXCL` copy that is synced and renamed; Windows renames the checked handle, opened without `FILE_SHARE_WRITE`, so a file another handle can still write is `busy`), `RingSpec {slot_bytes, slots}` (`layout()`, `file_bytes()` for the kind's `MappedFile.bytes`), `RingWriter::{create, open_sized (a 5C zero-filled file), write_slot, write_slot_with (in place), flush}`, `RingReader::{open_untrusted (pread only, size and links re-checked at every read), open_sealed (maps read-only), read_slot -> SlotState {Empty, Ready {seq, len}, Busy}}`, `encode_header`/`decode_header`. Layout: a 32-byte header (`FINSHM01`, version 1, reserved 0, slot_bytes u64, slots u32 ≤ 65,536), a `{seq, len}` table, and slots from a 64-byte-aligned offset; each slot is a seqlock (odd while it is written, even with release once done). Errors are `SHM_REFUSED {file, reason, cause?}`, with reason one of `link|notRegular|hardLinked|overCap|sizeMismatch|truncated|changed|header|protocol|notAbsolute|badName|missing|exists|busy|io` (exit 5 in `fin/src/output.rs`). `test-hooks` feature: `hooks::{mmap_calls, force_copy, before_rename}`. Tests: unit 9, `tests/ring.rs` 7 (including a two-process round trip with the test binary re-run as the writer), `tests/refusals.rs` 16 unix + 4 Windows, `tests/commit.rs` 8 unix + 1 Windows. Fuzz target `crates/fin-shm/fuzz` (`ring_header`, its own workspace, not a member). Deps: `memmap2 0.9.12` plus the workspace's existing `libc`/`windows-sys`/`serde_json`/`fin-core`

Probed by hand too: a lowercased and a non-verbatim path open, a real C: → E: commit takes the copy path and removes the source (8.3 names are off on that C:, so a short-name path is untested). Fixed: `paths_that_are_not_absolute_or_have_dot_segments_are_refused` built its `..` path with `join`, which drops `..` from a verbatim `\\?\` path on Windows (it saw `missing`); `check_path`'s unit test gained the Windows forms

All fields are `meta`: numbers, bools, closed enums or a `Name` from the new `MEDIA_CODECS` / `MEDIA_BACKENDS` tables (`names_media.rs`; `backend_class` is the spec §6.1 vendor class); an unknown value reads as `other`. **No adapter or driver name string and no path is recorded**: the GPU is its PCI vendor id, device id and the driver's major version (`driver_major`, the first number in wgpu's `driver_info`; `AdapterSummary` / `GpuAdapter` gained `driver_major`). **Emission**: `fin-workers/src/media/diag.rs`; the worker builds the `media` object into `LookStats.media` (the kind's `journal(job)` in `media.look.ts` passes it as `extra.media`; `jobDonePayload` lets only `media` through), sends `media.capabilities` from the `media.capabilities` job (once per server run: Task 13 caches the answer), and `render.stats` at the end of every job (what that job measured, even if it failed; several entries may share a window: readers add them). Decode time is measured around `FrameReader::frame` (`Sources.decodes`, per layer, with `FrameReader::backend()`), readback inside `Compositor::render` (`last_readback_micros()`), composite is the rest of `render` less those two. With `start.diagnostics` off nothing is measured into a payload, `stats.media` is absent and no `diag` is sent (`media_diag.rs` counts zero); `media.look`'s family declares `diagKinds: [media.capabilities, render.stats]`. A malformed `media` object makes the journal refuse the `job.done`; the sink then records it again without `media` (a worker cannot cost a job its `job.done`). Deviations: `rtFactor` is `rtFactorMilli` (integer thousandths; payloads carry no floats), no `profile` per codec (`fin-codecs`' capability table has none in 6A), `render.sample` / `preview.stats` have empty payloads (the registry test for training-only kinds skips reserved ones). ## Sub-project 8: plugins and registry (designed 2026-11-07, revision 2)

Spec: `docs/specs/2026-11-07-sub-project-8-plugins-design.md`. ### Sub-project 8A: packages, registries and lifecycle

Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-07-sub-project-8a-packages-and-registries.md`. Branch `sp8a-packages` (from `sp4c-edit-breadth`; rebased onto `sp5a-principals` after its Task 12, before Task 9a).

Spike in `archive/spikes/sp8a-tuf/` (git-ignored): `fin-node-spike.patch` (adds `tough`, `rustls` with `aws_lc_rs`, a second `ureq` client and two throwaway napi functions to `fin-node`; Windows x64 also gets `aws-lc-rs/prebuilt-nasm`), `tough-check/` (behaviour checks on repositories built with `tough`'s editor), `fetch.cjs`, logs. Results on macOS arm64:
  - **Build:** clean release `fin-node` with `PATH` holding only rustup, `rtk` and the system dirs (no CMake, NASM or Go): `aws-lc-sys` "Building with: CC", 1 min 37 s (unpatched: 1 min 23 s). Release `.node` (unstripped) 16,438,016 → 21,699,616 bytes, **+5.02 MiB** (limit 6 MiB). `cargo deny check licenses sources bans`: ok with the current `deny.toml`. `tough` depends on `rustls` with default features, so every build that links it has `rustls` with **both** `ring` and `aws_lc_rs`.
  - **HTTPS:** one `.node` fetched two public URLs (from the run's environment) through fin-fonts' client (`ring`) and a `ureq`/`rustls` client naming `aws_lc_rs`: both `Ok`, no panic, no process default before or after; `tough` loaded a local repository in the same `.node`. With both providers compiled in, `ClientConfig::builder()` without a provider panics (checked); fin-fonts' only config (`http::Http::new`) already names `ring`, so no code change. Kept: `crates/fin-fonts/tests/provider.rs` `no_process_default_provider` (both providers via a dev-dependency on `rustls` with `aws_lc_rs`; asserts an unnamed builder panics, drives a fetch through a loopback `CONNECT` proxy to a ClientHello, no default installed); removing the named provider makes it fail.
  - **Behaviour (17/17 checks pass, `tough-check.log`):** `ExpirationEnforcement::Unsafe` loads an expired repository and still refuses a bad timestamp signature, an unsigned body edit, a lower timestamp version, a lower snapshot version, a wrong target hash and a target over its signed length (`Safe` refuses the expired timestamp). Root chain 1→2→3 with root-key rotations: killed when `3.root.json` is fetched, the datastore holds root 2; resuming from it reaches root 3. Fast-forward recovery: a timestamp-key rotation deletes the trusted `timestamp.json` and `snapshot.json`, and timestamp v3 signed by the new key is accepted after v10 (without a rotation v3 is `OlderMetadata`).
  - **Gaps `fin-registry::tuf` must cover (Tasks 4a/4b):** (1) `tough`'s datastore writes are not atomic (`tokio::fs::write`, no temp/rename or fsync; `root.json` is removed, then written), and it writes `timestamp.json` before the snapshot verifies (and so on down the chain): give `tough` a scratch datastore per refresh seeded with copies of the trusted set, and promote the result temp-then-rename only after `load()` succeeds. (2) `tough` takes the trusted root from the caller, never from its datastore, and stores it re-serialised (1,787 bytes for a 2,391-byte root): keep our own `<n>.root.json` chain from the raw fetched bytes; the `Transport` adapter, when asked for `<n+1>.root.json`, first saves the `<n>.root.json` it served (verified by then), temp then rename. (3) Any transport error on `<n+1>.root.json` ends the root walk as "no newer root": the fs transport returns `FileNotFound` only for a missing file, and the adapter fails the refresh after any other error on a root. (4) When a saved root rotates the timestamp or snapshot keys, delete our trusted timestamp and snapshot in the same step (`tough` compares only with the root passed in). (5) `tough` reads the wall clock and writes `latest_known_time.json` (`SystemTimeSteppedBackward`): do not seed it into the scratch datastore; always load with `Unsafe` and apply both `strict` and `relaxed` expiry with the injected `Clock`. (6) Size caps: `Limits` has root, timestamp and snapshot caps and `max_root_updates` (set 32), but a role whose length the snapshot lists is read up to that length, so the adapter enforces Decision 15's per-file caps by name; the ≤ 500 delegations cap is ours
  - **Windows x64:** with NASM absent and no `prebuilt-nasm`, `aws-lc-sys` 0.47.0 stops ("NASM command not found! Build cannot continue."); with the feature it copies its bundled objects. Task 4a puts `aws-lc-rs = { version = "1", features = ["prebuilt-nasm"] }` under `cfg(all(windows, target_arch = "x86_64"))` in `fin-registry`

- [x] Task 3: `fin-registry::package` (no TUF code; depends on `fin-plugin-api`, `zip`, `blake3`, `sha2`, `unicode-normalization`): `unpack(archive, dest, executables, &Limits)` → `Unpacked {root, tree_hash, manifest, files: {path → FileMeta {size, sha256, executable}}}`. Pass 1 reads only the central directory: duplicate central-directory names (`zip` keeps one entry per name, so a second one would be invisible here), entry count, UTF-8 names (non-ASCII needs the UTF-8 flag), stored or deflate and not encrypted, regular files and directories only, the name rules, declared bytes ≤ 512 MiB, ratio ≤ 200:1 per entry, a root `finisher-plugin.json` ≤ 256 KiB. Pass 2 creates `dest` (it must not exist), reads the manifest for its executables, streams each file to a temp file in a private directory inside `dest` (named like no top-level entry), refuses anything but exactly the declared size, sets 0644/0755 (archive modes ignored), renames into place, creates directories one component at a time (each checked to be a real directory) and removes `dest` on any failure. Name rules (`check_path`, `check_component`): empty, `.`/`..`, leading `/`, `\`, NUL and control characters, `:` and Windows' other forbidden characters, reserved device names in any case and with any extension (CON… CLOCK$, COM0–9 and ¹²³, LPT likewise), trailing dot or space, components > 255 bytes, paths > 1,024, not NFC, case-folded duplicates (lower and upper case, files and directories alike), a file used as a directory. `tree_hash(root)` = BLAKE3 over sorted `path \0 class \0 sha256`, class `x` for the owner-execute bit (always `f` on Windows), empty directories add nothing. `pack(src, executables, out)` → `PackInfo {bytes, sha256, entries, tree_hash}`: files only, sorted, deflate level 6, DOS epoch, made by Unix (the `zip` writer otherwise writes DOS on Windows), 0644/0755 from the list, no extra fields; refuses what the reader would. Errors `{code, message, details}`: `PACKAGE_UNSAFE {entry, reason}`, `PACKAGE_TOO_LARGE {reason, limit, got}`, `PLUGIN_MANIFEST_INVALID {path, reason: missing|schema}`, `MANIFEST_TOO_LARGE`, `IO_ERROR`. Tests: 4 (`hostile_archive_corpus` with 45 refused cases plus entry, byte, manifest and corrupt-archive cases built byte by byte in `tests/support/hostile.rs`; `unpack_sets_modes_only_for_executables`; `pack_is_reproducible`; `tree_hash_changes_on_any_byte_or_mode`). As built: a lying declared size is caught by `zip` itself (`corrupt`) before the reader's own count (`sizeMismatch`); the "hard link" case is a second entry of one path (zip has no hard links; tools that keep them write duplicates)
- [x] Task 4a: `fin-registry::tuf` on `tough` 0.24 (`default-features = false`) and aws-lc-rs (`prebuilt-nasm` under `cfg(all(windows, target_arch = "x86_64"))`); every new crate is already in `tough`'s tree (`async-trait`, `bytes`, `futures`, `hex`, `jiff`, `olpc-cjson`, `pem`, `tempfile`, `tokio` `rt`, `url`: MIT and/or Apache-2.0, `idna`'s ICU crates Unicode-3.0), `cargo deny` ok. `TrustStore::new(<server-data>/plugins/trust, rootSha256)`: `roots/<n>.root.json` as fetched, `gen-<k>/{timestamp,snapshot,targets}.json` + `roles/<publisher>.json` behind a `current` pointer replaced atomically (a refresh promotes a whole generation or nothing), `state.json {lastRefreshAt}`, `tmp/` scratch. `TrustedRepo::{init(store, root, sha) (REGISTRY_UNTRUSTED; an existing store resumes), open, refresh(Arc<dyn Transport>, &Policy, &dyn Clock) -> RefreshReport, targets(), target(name), fetch_target(t, name, out), fetch_target_bytes, versions, max_stale_days, last_refresh_at}`; `TargetInfo {name, role, length, sha256, custom}` + `package() -> PackageCustom`. Gaps covered: `tough` gets a scratch datastore seeded with copies of the trusted timestamp and snapshot (never `latest_known_time.json`) and always loads `Unsafe`; the transport adapter applies Decision 15's caps by file name, saves `<n>.root.json` (the served bytes) before `tough` fetches `<n+1>`, drops the trusted timestamp and snapshot in the same step when that root changes their keys, and fails the refresh on any root-walk error but not-found; `checks.rs` adds consistent snapshots, one terminating `<publisher>/*` delegation per publisher (no top-level role names, no nested delegation, targets inside the prefix), ≤ 500 delegations, and no snapshot-listed role left unloaded. Targets are fetched by us (`<dir>/<sha256>.<base>`, the TUF 1.0 name; `tough`'s `<sha>.<dir/base>` is not used) with the signed length as cap, then length and SHA-256 checked. Errors `{code, message, details}` per spec §13 (`REGISTRY_UNTRUSTED|INVALID|UNREACHABLE`, `TUF_SIGNATURE|THRESHOLD|ROLLBACK|EXPIRED|TARGET_MISMATCH|DELEGATION`, `PLUGIN_NOT_FOUND`, `IO_ERROR`), the delegated role named. `transport::{Transport, TransportError {NotFound, TooLarge, BadPath, Unreachable}, FsTransport}` (regular files only, no symlinks). `editor::RepoBuilder::{init[_with](dir, keys_dir, root_keys, threshold, maxStaleDays[, clock]), open(dir, keys_dir), delegate, publish(pkg, key) (strict reader + `validate`, `PLUGIN_VERSION_EXISTS`), yank(id, version, key), advise(advisory, key), rotate(Role, keys, threshold), resign_timestamp(days)}`, signing canonical JSON (`olpc-cjson`) itself; `SigningKey`/`PublicKey` as PKCS#8 / SPKI PEM (OpenSSL's v1 documents parse), `KeyRing` (one PEM per key id, outside the repository); `advisory::{Advisory, AdvisoryFile}` (types only). Custom metadata `PackageCustom {id, version, kind, hostApi, platforms, license, permissionsDigest, manifestSha256, yanked?, bytesUnpacked}`; `permissions_digest` = SHA-256 of the sorted permission JSON (since the t8-11 merge: `target::permissions_digest`, BLAKE3; see the merge note below). As built (beyond the plan): `refresh` takes `Arc<dyn Transport>` (`tough` needs a `'static` transport); `RepoBuilder` takes a separate `keys_dir` (keys never inside the published folder) and holds the online keys there; `yank` takes the publisher's key (only that role can change its targets); `delegate` records the publisher and the delegation is signed into `targets` with its first role, because `tough` 0.24 loads **no** delegated role when one has no file (an early `return` in `load_delegations`); re-delegation is `rotate(Role::Publisher)`, which re-signs the role with the new key in the same commit. Fixed vectors: `tests/fixtures/tuf/vectors/{basic,rotated}` (`test-only` publisher, keys discarded; regenerate with `FIN_REGEN_TUF_VECTORS=1`). Tests: 9 (`tuf_happy` 6, `editor` 3)

an approval reads the plan row (`PLAN_EXPIRED {missing|expired}`, `HUMAN_ONLY {reason: planMaker}` for a plan a non-human made, whoever submits it, `HUMAN_ONLY {reason: approver}` again for a non-human approver), checks the digest and the base (`PLAN_MISMATCH {reason: installed}`), commits through fin-registry with the installed directories, then in one `control.db` transaction re-checks the base, writes the row and the grants as planned and deletes the plan; a person's also updates content packs with `autoUpdate` on when the plan is an update of content that is not agent-facing, widens nothing (`added`, `widened`, `riskUp`), carries no `thirdPartyCode`, `yanked` or `disable` advisory warning and is permissive, else the plan is discarded), the sweep (expired plan rows and fin-registry's staging, at start and every minute), migration `plugins/0002.ts` (`plugin_suggestions`). Deviations: (1) M2's "one transaction": the Rust commit runs on fin-node's worker thread, and a SQLite transaction held open across that await would take unrelated writers on the connection (audit rows, credentials) into it and roll them back with it, so the lifecycle lock isolates the steps and the base is re-checked before the commit and again inside the row transaction; (2) `autoUpdate` is set by `plugin.install {autoUpdate}` (no RPC listed one; kept on updates when absent); (3) the agent-facing rule is carried by `InstallPlan.agentFacing`, set in tests through `testAgentFacing`; (4) 5E's review items have not landed, so suggestions are `plugin_suggestions` rows (follow-up below); (5) a plan's refresh applies `disable` advisories but never auto-updates
